travel carries zeroed with speed and its carry kept; the clears; the different-cell restart; three causes opening no hold; the zero-horizon replay of the old body); the invariant walk asserts `ever_held`; `a_stop_a_wreck_and_a_cold_hull_all_zero_the_carries` runs its `Occupied` row at 0 with the reason at the site. `hash.rs` probes cell alone and stamp alone. `tests/arena.rs`: `Reading` alias and `another_unit_holds_a_tank_for_two_seconds_and_then_stops_it_with_one_line` (silence, one `Held: something is in the way.` at or after `first_held + 2_000`, frame `1_000`/false/no line during, `0`/true after, one `blocked` line); the cross-tempo script's `blocked` count 2 to 4. `tests/reflex.rs`: `causeway`, `one_way_west`, `column_of(n)` with `in_column(n) = ObjId(200 - n)` (lowest id at the rear), `road(len, node)`, `road_home(len, zone_x, node)`, `Detour`, `convoy_and_wreck` / `the_leader_is_wrecked`; the two-machine ford, the six-machine column, the parked-head measurement (8 matured stops, 5 of 6 learned, last through 34,000 ms after release), the wreck held two seconds then learned, `B-42` braking (top 1,500, 415 cm/s at the window, west-most cell 5, standing at the end), the rally clock on `road_home` (west-most cell 10, no restarts, fails with the profile reverted), `a_routeless_machine_is_ordered_to_stop_within_one_substep_of_a_stop` (250 ms after the stop, 996 ms off the decide grid, intent counted; fails with the pre-pass call removed), the mandatory cross-tempo `the_convoy_and_the_wreck_are_one_event_list_and_one_hash_sequence_at_three_cadences` at 5,000 / 1,000 / 500 ms, `a_ragged_partition_...` extended with `stopped_at >= since_ms + 2_000` and `< + 250` per run, `dry_west > 4_500`. `reflex.rs` unit tests: the `B-43` overflow half and `e3_the_pre_pass_holds_a_routeless_machine_and_skips_everyone_else` (seven cases, `(desired_speed_cms, intents)`). `tests/extensions.rs`: `e3_a_routeless_machine_is_held_off_the_cliff_every_substep` (no `blocked` over 30 s on `plateau(false)`, header states the pre-pass fires zero times there). `tests/shipped_scale.rs`: the duel pins `speed_cms <= 1_200` at the shot (measured 1,097), zero within 7,000 ms (measured 6,750) and never above zero while in `Engage`; `e3_two_machines_of_one_side_cross_the_ford_without_stopping_each_other` (0 stops, nothing remembered, east-most 130,288 cm over 240 s). `tests/harness.rs`: `blocked_cells_of`, `Counts::blocked_at`, `harness_arena_blocked_split: interior= ring= impassable=` and JSON `blocked_interior_passable` / `blocked_ring_passable` / `blocked_impassable`. `mushd/tests/observer_rule.rs`: `e3_the_traffic_stops_one_line_names_nothing_and_places_nothing`. Harness (same tree, before at `occupied_stop_ms: 0` / after at 2,000): blocked 348 to 326, interior 58 to 29 (halved), ring 0 / 0, impassable 290 to 297; fired 776 to 773, hits 700 / 700, units destroyed 13 to 14, ticks 8,916 to 8,902, intents 18,587 to 18,525, traces 253 to 251, path cells 68,931 to 95,251 (peak 7,407 to 18,212); p99 6.325 before, 6.907 to 7.920 after on an unloaded box and 11.315 on the gate's loaded one, all under the 12 ms lens; every simulation counter byte-identical across builder, fixer and gate runs. **E.4** (`b18ee30d`): `ReflexUnit` gains three hashed fields: `last_plot: Option<((u16, u16), i64)>` (the cell of the plot the tick acted on and `t_ms`; written last in `decide` by `Approach`/`Engage` from `plot_of(id, contact)` and by a `Withdraw` that stood, through a `stood_cell` local carrying `withdraw_stand`'s plot because `Withdraw` clears `row.target` on entry; an off-window plot writes nothing), `unheld_since_ms: Option<i64>` and `hunt: Option<(u16, u16)>`; `state_hash` folds them after the `rallied` byte (presence byte, `x`/`y` u16 LE, stamp i64 LE via `fold_stamp`), and `each_new_row_field_moves_the_state_hash` gains six probes including cell-alone and stamp-alone. `Patrol`'s goal is now a list in order, written into `ReflexState::Patrol`'s doc and the module head: a movement order, the search (`Arena::search_goal`: drive to `last_plot` while its age is at most `reflex.search_ms` (inclusive, so the validated floor `search_ms == decide_ms` is one tick of search and not `0` under another name) and the machine is outside `ARRIVE_CELLS`), the hunt (`Arena::hunt_goal`: `holds_a_hostile` holds the clock at `None` and clears `hunt`; else `unheld_since_ms.get_or_insert(t_ms)` and at `reflex.hunt_after_ms` the goal is `Arena::far_entry_cell(id)`), then `patrol_goal` unchanged. Clears: arrival, age or `search_ms == 0` clear `last_plot`; both `Withdraw` entries (the order's `Objective::Withdraw` arm and the health/dry edge) clear `last_plot`; a `movement` guard at the head of the `Patrol` arm (a live `Advance` or `Hold`) clears `last_plot` and `hunt` and restarts `unheld_since_ms` on every ordered tick so neither clock's goal survives an order and the wait counts from its end; every tick whose end state is not `Patrol` clears `hunt` and restarts the clock; `follow`'s `Err` arm beside E.2's order clear drops a failed search goal (`last_plot = None`) or a failed hunt goal (`hunt = None`, clock restarted) so each costs exactly one failed search; a hunt arrival also forgets `row.waypoint`/`row.from_waypoint` so the walk resumes from the node nearest the far edge; `ReflexMemory::adopt` clears all three. `far_entry_cell` is new and private, `entry_cell` has no diff: over entry zones whose `team != unit.team` in zone order, the clamped corner when passable else the nearest passable cell by Manhattan distance found by a scan reading rows nearest the unit's clamped row first (`cy`, `cy-1`, `cy+1`, ...) under one shared `MAX_ENTRY_SCAN` budget, the nearest candidate wins with ties to the lower zone index, and it never answers a refused cell: budget exhausted or no far zone answers `None`, on which `hunt_goal` restarts the clock so the scan costs once per `hunt_after_ms`; the scan is deliberately duplicated from `entry_cell` (its `.or(Some(clamped))` shape cannot be shared without changing an answer). Config: `[tacsim.arena.reflex].search_ms` (u32, default 60000, refusal verbatim `[tacsim.arena.reflex].search_ms is 0 (off) or {decide_ms} to 600000; {value} is not`) and `hunt_after_ms` (u32, default 90000, refusal `[tacsim.arena.reflex].hunt_after_ms is 0 (off) or at least {decide_ms}; {value} is not`) on `tacsim::config::ReflexConfig` and `mush_config::config::TacsimReflexCfg`, mapped in `config_ext::tacsim_config`, round-tripped (30000/45000), three refusal rows and five boots-fine rows, carried as 60000/90000 by `mushd.toml.example`, `deploy/instance/mushd.toml.tmpl` and `deploy/dev2/mushd.toml` with the brief's comments, and `shipped_configs.rs` asserts each clock is 0 or at least `decide_ms` in every shipped file and 60000/90000 in the rendered instances and the example. `T-42`: `e4_t42_a_round_stopped_by_the_ground_re_plans_the_machine` is the first test to reach `Arena::note_no_line` (500 ms breech, `curtain_with_a_gap` leaving row 0 open, a `damage::BLOCKED` miss, `Approach` with `no_line` cleared and the goal the plot's cell); `e4_t42_a_routeless_machine_holds_at_a_cell_it_was_stopped_at` covers the `avoid` clause through `note_blocked(.., Occupied, ..)` (`0 cm/s`, driving again past `REFLEX_AVOID_TTL_MS`), and `first_step_is_open`'s doc now says it is the search's edge rule plus the remembered stops, stricter than the crossing (`motion::refusal` applies no shoulder rule); `e4_t42_the_spent_draw_is_one_log_at_three_cadences` replays `both_sides_dry` (ends at 0 ms, documented), `one_round_in_flight` and the new `both_fire_their_last` (ends `Spent` at 13,250 ms) at 5,000/1,000/500 ms with fired-count, end-time, hash-length and differing-pulses guards. Help: the E.4 sentence after E.1's doctrine paragraph in `docs/help/simulator.md`, baked into the bot primer. Rig (`tests/support/mod.rs`): `Wear { Hurt, Dry }`, `Muster::wear` with `hurt()`/`dry()` applied in `shipped_arena` before `Arena::new`; `hurt_to_withdraw` (one `UnitMutationPlan` through `mush_tactics::apply`, every facet `armor` cell to `min + 1`, no internals, 227 per mille on the line tank, measured and asserted) and `shot_dry` (every component `ammo` cell to its floor, rows kept so the tank is `is_dry` and armed); `Timeline` with `TIMELINE_EVERY_MS = 10_000`, `sample` and `render` (per unit per 10 s: state, cell, goal, target, health per mille, field-wide `path_failures`). The case, recorded at HEAD `c3852a0c` before any fix: hurt 1 v 2 ended `Elimination { Blue }` at 349,750 ms, healthy 1 v 1 `Elimination { Red }` at 341,750 ms, and the dry loser did not end in 900,000 ms (dry Red #3 parked on its entry cell (6, 97) in `Withdraw` from 20 s, Blue #7 walking the east half of the map at 300/600/900 s, 0 path failures), which is 16c `B-04`'s cause observed. After: 195,750 / 188,750 / 286,750 ms, all by elimination, 2.2 s of debug wall for the four shipped-scale cases so all stay ordinary tests and the CI inventory did not move. `tests/shipped_scale.rs` gains `ENDGAME_MS = 900_000`, `play_to_the_end`, `ended_by_elimination` and the three `e4_*` cases; `tests/reflex.rs` gains `E4_SIGHT_CELLS = 7`, `e4_cfg`, `e4_field`, `e4_pinned`, `e4_units`, `e4_definition`, `red_zone`, `blue_zone`, `e4_run_until_state`, `e4_run_until_hunt`, `e4_order`/`e4_advance`/`e4_run_until_unordered`, `a_search_and_a_hunt`, `Arc`/`e4_the_arc_at_500`, `curtain_with_a_gap`, `both_fire_their_last`, `Replay::pulses` (counted by all three replay constructors) and sixteen new tests including the mandatory cross-tempo arc contact, lost, search, arrival, quiet, hunt, far-zone arrival, contact at three cadences; a unit test `adopt_forgets_the_search_and_the_hunt`. Harness after (before in brackets): ticks 8,918 (8,902), intents 18,574 (18,525), shots 708 (698), path_queries 1,006 (1,008), path_cells 87,008 (95,251), peak_path_cells 10,171 (18,212), path_failures 0 (0), traces 253 (251); states patrol 174 (175), approach 4 (7), engage 106 (97), withdraw 4 (5), disabled 12 (16); fired 783 (773), hits 710 (700), units destroyed 11 (14); p99 7.454 ms (7.214 ms). The hunt cannot fire inside the harness's 41.5 s; the search made the field cheaper, a near goal replacing a far node. **The record**: the commit carrying this row fills it, writes the doc 16 amendments the four sub-cuts owed (in place in sections 3.1, 3.3, 3.4 and 6, as parentheses on the F.3, Q.7 and D.1 entries of section 11, and as four as-built entries there), marks 16c (ten bugs and six debts closed, `T-42` closed in part, the cut's landing line and the deferrals in section 4; no row added), corrects the `tempo.alert_range_cm` row of the constants table above and adds the cut's four keys to it, and adds the post-E baseline row. | **The loop**, once per sub-cut and strictly serial, cut D's loop unchanged: a builder working from a written brief cut from the plan and from the judge's surface text of the sub-cut before; independent lenses on the uncommitted diff; a fixer who fixed or rebutted every finding; an independent gate that owned cargo and edited nothing; a judge who read the whole diff against the brief and this row and wrote the landed, deviations, residual and surface texts; and an agent commit AND push only on a green gate and a ship verdict. The plan went through the loop first: revision 2 was written after a feasibility and an adversarial review, 23 findings each re-checked against the code at `512e1be1` (18 adopted, 5 adopted in part, none rejected whole; the plan's section 8 is the disposition table), and every 16c row was re-verified at that HEAD with none found already fixed. The orchestrator's first run carried E.1 and stopped at E.2 with no judge verdict recorded; the E.2 brief's `B-41` section was rewritten against the two review findings that had replaced the sketch (F/E-04 and A/E-08: the order ends for the unit rather than the cell being remembered), and a second run carried E.2, E.3, E.4 and the sweep. It never halted on a verdict: all four judges ruled ship-with-notes, and all four commits are pushed. **Findings, where the judges' texts give them.** No judge's text for this cut gives a must-fix count. What they name: at E.1 the final gate's clippy step replayed a warm cache that postdated the newest source, so no lint work was re-executed in the gate itself; at E.2 the fixer saw one load flake in `mushd --test observer_rule` (two leftover lock lines in a session read), green on every rerun; at E.3 every simulation counter was byte-identical across the builder's, the fixer's and the gate's runs, and the gate's box was loaded (its p99 below); at E.4 the fixer diagnosed the ford orbit of a lone hunter in water from the hurt 1 v 2 timeline and did not fix it (steering, out of scope: it is in the residuals). **The harness at each sub-cut's gate** (300 units, the defaults 24/8, `TACSIM_ASSERT=1`, engaged p99 against cut S's 12 ms, cut D.4's record being 7.421 ms, 761 fired, 684 hits, 14 units destroyed): E.1 7.363 ms (p50 1.216 / p95 6.909 / max 7.739; 757 fired, 682 hits, 9 units destroyed, `blocked` 294, reflex traces 251, path cells 135,648 to 86,638, `alert_range_cm` 500,000 to 362,000); E.2 7.162 (1.049 / 6.917 / 7.413; 767 fired, 691 hits, 76 misses, 9 destroyed, path cells 110,811, path failures 0); E.3 11.315 on the gate's loaded box, the same tree reading 6.907 to 7.920 unloaded with every counter identical (2.268 / 10.837 / 11.847 loaded; at the key flip on one tree `blocked` 348 to 326, interior traffic stops 58 to 29, ring 0, impassable 290 to 297, fired 776 to 773, hits 700, destroyed 13 to 14, path cells 68,931 to 95,251 with the peak 7,407 to 18,212); E.4 7.454 (1.090 / 6.659 / 7.457; 783 fired, 710 hits, 11 destroyed, path cells 87,008 with the peak 10,171, end states patrol 174 / approach 4 / engage 106 / withdraw 4 / disabled 12). Every sub-cut moved the simulation counters, as the plan said it would, because the whole field of 300 is on the controller; no pinned hash or digest existed to move and none moved. Every sub-cut added its three-cadence cross-tempo test (`e1_the_band_taken_and_given_up_is_one_event_list_at_three_cadences`; E.2's meshed-pair kill under an expiring withdraw order and its unreachable `Advance` with a hot gun; `the_convoy_and_the_wreck_are_one_event_list_and_one_hash_sequence_at_three_cadences` with `a_ragged_partition_...` extended across a hold's maturity; E.4's contact, lost, search, arrival, quiet, hunt, far-zone arrival, contact, and `e4_t42_the_spent_draw_is_one_log_at_three_cadences`). **The shipped-scale cases** (`tests/shipped_scale.rs`, the production roster on the authored proving ground, no pilots): at E.1 the duel's first round was fired at 169,250 ms inside 600 m (40,911 cm) by a machine in `Engage` ordered to 0 while rolling 1,003 cm/s, stopped within ten seconds, elimination at 199,750 ms, 0.54 s for the binary in debug; at E.3 the first shot is at 1,097 cm/s and the shooter reads 0 at 6,750 ms; at E.4, run at `c3852a0c` before any fix, the hurt 1 v 2 ended `Elimination { Blue }` at 349,750 ms, the healthy 1 v 1 `Elimination { Red }` at 341,750 ms and the dry loser did not end in 900,000 ms (dry Red #3 parked on its entry cell (6, 97) in `Withdraw` from 20 s, Blue #7 walking the east half of the map, 0 path failures: 16c `B-04`'s cause observed), and after the search and the hunt 195,750 / 188,750 / 286,750 ms, all by elimination, 2.2 s of debug wall for the four cases, so all stay ordinary tests. **The full sweep**, twice. Round 1 on `b18ee30d` (E.4's commit): `cargo fmt --all -- --check` and `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean; `cargo test --workspace --exclude mush-bot --no-fail-fast` 312 test binaries and 43 doc-test targets, 5,711 passed, 0 failed, 79 ignored, no LNK1104 and no hang; `cargo test -p mush-bot` failed once, `--test life :: quiet_work_study_cycle_uses_ssh_and_restores_receipts_without_replaying_work` (`create_dir` `AlreadyExists`, os error 183, at `life.rs:73`: the `temp()` fixture named its directory by pid and `SystemTime` nanos alone, two async tests in one binary share a pid and the Windows clock ticks in 100 ns steps), green on two reruns of the test and on a full rerun of the crate; the web steps (`npm ci`, 454 vitest tests, the build, `ts_export`, a clean proto diff) green; the harness 2 passed with engaged p99 6.595 ms and ground tempo p99 3.873. The one repair, `145296c2` (not a cut E defect: `life.rs` was last touched in `5da8d6a3`): the `temp()` fixtures of `life.rs`, `playtesting.rs` (six callers in one binary, the same race) and `social_campaign.rs` (one caller, hardened) take a function-local `AtomicU64` counter as a third name component, the idiom `campaign.rs` already used; no assertion changed; every other pid-plus-time fixture in the workspace was checked and none can collide. Round 2 on `145296c2`, the tree clean before and after: fmt and clippy clean; the workspace totals identical (5,711 / 0 / 79 across 312 binaries and 43 doc-test targets: binaries 5,693 / 0 / 76, doc-tests 18 / 0 / 3); `mush-bot` 262 passed, 0 failed, 0 ignored on its first run; web 454 vitest tests passed; the harness 2 passed: `harness_arena_engaged: period_ms=500 pulses=60 p50=1.047 p95=6.285 p99=6.848 max=7.440`, ground `harness_tempo_current: period_ms=2000 pulses=60 p50=3.369 p95=4.067 p99=4.370 max=4.593`, and every deterministic counter equal to round 1's: 783 fired, 783 impacts, 710 hits, 73 misses, 423 rounds spent, 33 sections and 11 units destroyed, 11 wrecks and 3 disabled at the end; `blocked` 325 (interior 27, ring 0, impassable 298), 133 moving and 26 held at the end, 0 lagged pulses, 2 substeps of 250 ms; reflex 8,918 ticks, 18,574 intents, 708 shots, 1,006 path queries, 655 deferred, 87,008 path cells (peak 10,171), 0 failures, 253 traces; perception peak 299 passes, 5,428 candidates, 2,525 traces (bound 4,000), 34,078 probes, 218 share operations (bound 256); perception total 1,444 detected, 986 classified, 1,267 lost, 940 contacts at the end; 1,435,041 ledger bytes in 89 chunks, 23.07 frames per pulse; `alert_range_cm` 362,000. The harness also prints `cut_a_p99_ms=5`, which the engaged p99 is above in both rounds; it gates nothing (the run passes under `assert=true`, and the binding lens is cut S's 12 ms). The post-E baseline row above records the sweep. The docs vocabulary gate (`cargo test -p mush-contract-tests --test hex_vocabulary`) was run green on the tree carrying this row, the doc 16 amendments and the 16c marks. **NOT run.** A hand-played match on this build: nothing of cut E has been flown by a person, the machines' new doctrine (the 420 m close, the release, the stand's two rules, the traffic hold, the braking, the search and the hunt) has been seen only by its tests and the harness, and no deploy of cut E to dev1 is part of this record (server half only, no pack, so a server-half restart with the world kept takes it; not done without asking the owner). The harness at the AUTHORED reaches of 60 sensor and 30 share cells, the owner's open choice since cut S, unmeasured since X.6. A resident-memory reading (absent on the Windows dev box). A cold clippy at E.1's gate. `mush-server --test provisioning_smoke` at E.2 (no key, template or config moved there; E.1, E.3 and E.4 ran it). The whole-workspace sweep by E.3's own gate (the brief's list plus `mush-config` and `config_ext` were run there; the cut sweep above covers it). Deliberation against a real provider: the `T-56` default and the `B-41` `order_expired` signal are exercised by tests alone. The second-stop rule's justifying measurement beyond the six-machine parked-head fixture. | **E.1.** Judge ship-with-notes. (1) Q9 departed from as planned: the fighting reach is the lesser of the nominal sight and the loaded weapon's band, not the inner band alone, because 1,500 m is past every plot and would park a machine that has range and no line. (2) Q8 departed from as planned: a machine that is home, or has no home (`(_, None)` counts as arrived), shoots back inside `release_cm` at any health, because one that never replies is the parked target cut Q.7 removed. (3) `fight_inner_cm` reads a new `WeaponSummary::inner_band_cm` rather than `bands_cm().first()`, to avoid a `Vec` per viewer per pulse in `hostile_in_weapons_range`; the equality is pinned for every shipped weapon. (4) `stand_cm` carries one cap the brief's formula did not, `.min(cap_inner)`, from review: the `close` band is a third of the last edge and `release_cm` is the first, so a short-first-band gun would otherwise stand where `engage_orders` refuses to fire; no shipped or fixture number moves. (5) The `Approach` in-band test keeps HEAD's `.max(1)` so a degenerate `engage_cm` of 0 still admits a machine standing on its plot. (6) The mandatory cross-tempo test does not replay the brief's wall-hidden close-on-the-plot leg: with the fixture cannon `release_cm` is the sight and a plot moves only when a pass refreshes it, so past-the-release-edge and behind-a-wall cannot be one scripted moment; the fixture uses a five-cell coaxial, the tank opens the range in the open, the arc is asserted pulse by pulse before the cadences are compared, and the no-line arm stays pinned at one cadence; E.2's own cross-tempo test is where a planted no-line fixture belongs. (7) The per-order midpoint hysteresis is behavioural only without an order; under `close` the test proves the seven-tenths engage edge, the ceiling and a parked target never fired on against a no-band control, and `effective` and `far` are arithmetic only because the 16,000 cm deliberation window cannot hold their midpoints. (8) The duel's first-shot triple is read at the first pulse end at or after the shot's millisecond (the hook runs when a pulse ends, and the next decide tick is a second later, so the state read is the firing tick's), and the ten-second zero-speed claim is conditional on the shooter still being in `Engage`, as the brief words it. (9) Fixtures changed and said so at each site: the deliberation field has two named scopes, `WIDE_SIGHT_CELLS` 35 for the ordered-band tests and `NARROW_SIGHT_CELLS` 3 for the two expiry tests, whose assertions are unchanged from HEAD; `a_cornered_withdrawal_...` moved from the carbine to the cannon (a third of 2,000 cm is under one cell) and now counts shots fired during the withdrawal only; `hurt` is split into `hurt` (166) and `hurt_but_fighting` (277, asserted); `withdraw_and_rally` plants its contact at 0 because the 1,666 cm stand band closes before `classify_after_ms`; `under_the_health_threshold_...` runs to 24,000 ms instead of 32,000 and measures west from the moment of the damage, because a machine under the floor no longer stops and so gets home and rallies sooner; `a_classified_hostile_out_of_reach_...` now pins the exact one-cell-floor goal (12,8). (10) `STAND_OFF_MARGIN_CELLS` cannot cover a band shorter than three cells; it cannot arise on shipped content (42 cells) and is recorded for a future short gun. (11) Not done: `engage_band_permille`'s key doc still says a weapon's reach at four sites and does not carry the 1000-is-one-distance sentence the brief asked for; `stand_off_cell` keeps its off-window fallback sentence because it is still true of the code; the paired boot refusal row asserts one of its two key names. (12) The brief's band word is read against `fire_bands_cm` (ammunition-blind) while an order's ceiling is priced off the loaded weapon's bands; identical on shipped content, where the sight caps both. (13) Doc 16 sentences now untrue and left for the closeout: section 3.1's `Engaged` row (weapons range is `fight_inner_cm`) and `Alert` row with 16b section 9's `tempo.alert_range_cm` row (the diagonal cap), section 6's machine sentence (three `Approach` cases, the release edge, two stand rules with the en-route ceiling the lesser of the `close` band and the firing ceiling), section 6's Q.7 cornered-unit paragraph (now conditional), and the utility-score sentence (the range term's denominator is `fight_limit_cm`). **E.2.** Judge ship-with-notes. (1) Doc 16 section 3.3's "a wreck is an obstacle and a contact, never a target" is now true of the world and not of every reader: a machine holding a stale track treats the contact as a target for up to `lost_after_passes x pass_ms` plus the loss hysteresis and may spend a burst on it, which is the brief's stated cost; the committer amends the sentence. (2) Doc 16 section 6's "an order expiring unrenewed drops the squad to doctrine" is incomplete: an `Advance` whose cell no route reaches ends the order for that unit at the tick its search failed, and the `unreachable` edge's "falls back to the nearest waypoint" no longer says that nothing re-asserts the cell; owed to the committer. (3) The bridge's `mind link dropped` sentence is keyed on the order's nominal time to live and not on the `order_expired` line, so a B-41 drop is never announced to the squad's pilots if a replacement order arrives first and is announced late otherwise; the brief said leave the bridge alone, and the remedy is a doc 16 section 6 sentence and a later cut. (4) Brief item 1(b)'s teammate proof is two tests rather than one: the production-path kill test's second machine holds its own sighting (the mesh is off in that file's stock fixture) and a separate test gives a mesh-only receiver behind a curtain of wall, with the kill planted the way the writer writes it because a scripted `destroy_unit` bypasses the notice scan; the cross-tempo replay carries four units not three because section 3.8's elimination would end the match at the kill. (5) Item 7's "a default above `MAX_HORIZON_MS` is clamped" cannot be built as stated because `MAX_TTL_MS == MAX_HORIZON_MS` and the signal clamps first; asserted as a default of `MAX_TTL_MS` yielding `MAX_HORIZON_MS` plus a stated horizon above it clamped. (6) `contact_reads_dead` lives in `perception.rs` beside `contact_within_alert_range`, not `reflex.rs`; the brief named no file. (7) Two B-41 pins are asserted looser than the brief phrased them: the line's timestamp inside two seconds of the order rather than at the failing tick's millisecond, and the fallback goal a waypoint at the end of the run without the range to it falling; the B-40 test does not read `row.route` and the B-45 rally test does not run the out-of-sight case on to expiry. (8) The predicate's three-second cost bound holds only under `sees_wrecks: false`; a sensor authored with `sees_wrecks: true` gives a hold on a wreck that is refreshed for ever, and no shipped sensor sets it. (9) The harness's reflex path cells rose 86,638 to 110,811 with path failures 0, consistent with dead targets held three seconds longer but not bisected; nothing asserts on it. (10) `tests/extensions.rs`'s cold-gun test was re-fixtured (`heat.per_s = 0`) beyond the assertion change the brief listed, because under T-55 a hull shedding three a second made the run a test of which substep the decide tick lands in; the "one shot short" pin became `heat_now == heat_max`. (11) No test was added in `tests/resolution.rs`; the proofs fit `reflex.rs`, `deliberation.rs`, `perception.rs` and `extensions.rs`. (12) A `SignalReason` that says why the order ended is not built, as the design note leaves it. (13) The hash probe's negative half uses the file's `if let Some(unit) = unit_mut(..)` idiom and would compare an arena with itself if the fixture were renumbered. (14) Helper names now collide across test binaries: `hurt_to_withdraw` in `tests/deliberation.rs` against the E.4 plan's `tests/support/mod.rs` helper, and `POCKET` at (10, 8) in `deliberation.rs` and (13, 13) in `reflex.rs`. (15) `mush-server --test provisioning_smoke` was not run: no key, template or config moved. (16) A load flake in `mushd --test observer_rule` (`nothing_about_an_undetected_unit_crosses_on_any_surface`, two leftover lock lines in a session read) was seen once by the fixer and green on every rerun; worth the load-sensitive list. **E.3.** Judge ship-with-notes. (1) Doc 16 section 3.4 step 4 alone is now untrue for the first `occupied_stop_ms` and only for `BlockedCause::Occupied`: its sentence "impassable for `tracked`, occupied by another unit, or off the window stops the unit at the boundary with `speed = 0` and one `blocked` notice per stop" now holds of ground, edge and slope at once and of traffic only after a hold on one cell outlasts `occupied_stop_ms` of arena time; the amendment is the committer's. The brief and the builder's report also named step 5, but step 5 reads in full "Occupancy is one unit per cell at 10 m cells; there is no ramming and no collision damage." and carries no stopping clause, so it and the invariant sentence are untouched (the invariant is asserted over the random walk with a parked hull in it). (2) The help sentence is inserted verbatim but after "...so a bank is never a one-way door." instead of after "...the bank onto it will not." as the brief specified, because at the brief's point it stranded "That last one" (the slope reference) and the only other repair changed a cockpit sentence, which the brief forbids. (3) The `B-42` arrival speed is 415 cm/s and asserted `<= 500`, not the brief's `<= 100`: the profile is the maximum-braking curve and a 500 ms decide tick leaves up to a tick of it unshed at the window edge; the overshoot half (west-most cell 5, never past the window) and the standing-still half are asserted as asked. (4) The duel's first-shot speed is 1,097 cm/s asserted `<= 1_200` (not at or below 1,000) and the shooter reads zero at 6,750 ms asserted within 7,000 (not 5,000): 1,097 cm/s at 200 cm/s squared is five and a half seconds and the `speed 0` order was given the tick before the shot; the never-above-zero claim is asserted as written. (5) The plateau test asserts no `blocked` line over 30 s but not that `ReflexCounters::intents` shows the holds, because the pre-pass fires zero times on `plateau(false)` (measured `blocked=0 intents=40 ticks=30`): the bearing to the sealed goal runs over open ground to the seam and cut Q.7's tick-time check plus the braking profile close the row on their own; `B-44` is closed instead on `a_routeless_machine_is_ordered_to_stop_within_one_substep_of_a_stop` (confirmed red with the `hold_routeless` call removed) and the guards unit test, and the plateau test's header says so. (6) The column fixtures put the lowest id at the REAR where the brief said "ids ASCENDING toward the rear": units integrate in id order, so the brief's own parenthetical and its `B-46` paragraph describe lowest-at-rear, which is the real worst case, and the parked-head numbers (8 matured, 5 of 6 learned, 34,000 ms) were measured under it. (7) `desired_speed_cms == 0` is tested inside the `Occupied` arm after the maturity test as well as cleared at the top of `advance`, so a coasting unit starts no clock it can never run out; the consequence, undisclosed by the builder, is that a unit ordered to speed 0 never matures an occupancy stop and so journals no `blocked` line and teaches no lesson where HEAD did, which is one input `note_blocked` lost. (8) A traffic hold keeps the heading and the way, so a unit refused on a diagonal heading slides into the diagonal neighbour within a substep or two rather than waiting; every crossing is still tested, terrain refusals are unchanged (16c section 6's "sliding along an obstacle after a terrain refusal" is still not built), it is written in `hold_at_boundary`'s doc, and it is why `a_stop_a_wreck_and_a_cold_hull_all_zero_the_carries` runs its `Occupied` row at 0. (9) A refusal of a different cell restarts the clock (the brief's rule), so a heading that changes every substep between two occupied neighbours holds indefinitely with no line, notice or lesson; pinned by `a_refusal_of_a_different_cell_restarts_the_clock` and bounded in the shipped lane because nothing changes a heading every substep. (10) The wreck-in-the-ford margin is `met_at + 1_500` (1,750 is the provable bound with 250 ms substeps inside 500 ms pulses). (11) The braking range is planar, matching `stand_off_cell`. (12) `dry_west > 5_000` lowered to `> 4_500` (measured 4,990) because braking for the entry cell costs a fixed twenty seconds a little ground; the discriminating clause `> armed_west + 3_000` is untouched. (13) The help's "at the speed it had" is approximate: `accelerate` runs before the crossing test, so a held tank's speed keeps climbing toward its order while the pose stands. (14) The harness split was built by classifying cells (three buckets named for what the window can answer) and the `occupied_stop_ms = 0` comparison was run as well; both runs read `ring=0`, so no edge stop was ever inside the traffic figure. (15) Against the brief's expectation, path cells rose 38 per cent (a held column reaches the ford together and re-plans there) and p99 rose about 0.5 ms over E.1's record on the builder's box; the before-run with the pre-pass on and the hold off reads 6.325, so the pre-pass was not cheapened. (16) The second-stop rule is not built, as the plan directs; the parked-head measurement is the evidence. (17) `first_step_is_open`'s doc is left misstating the shoulder rule for E.4's `T-42`. (18) The validation sentence the operator sees is `[tacsim.arena].occupied_stop_ms must be 0 to 60000 (it is 60001)`, from the shared `between` helper, not the sentence the builder's report quoted. (19) The whole-workspace sweep was not run by builder, fixer or gate. **E.4.** Judge ship-with-notes. (1) Doc 16 §6 needs two amendments, which are the committer's since the builder edits nothing under docs/design: the machine sentence `Patrol (the arena pack's waypoint graph)` is now four answers in order (a movement order, the search, the hunt, the waypoint graph), and the as-built tail should gain E.4's clause: a machine that loses a contact drives to its own last plot for `reflex.search_ms`, and one that has held nothing hostile for `reflex.hunt_after_ms` crosses to another side's entry zone by `far_entry_cell`. (2) The search's age test is inclusive (`t_ms - at > search_ms` clears) where the brief wrote `t_ms - at < search_ms` (searches), because the plot is always exactly `decide_ms` old when the first `Patrol` tick reads it, so under the brief's letter the validated floor `search_ms == decide_ms` would silently be `0`; the validation band and refusal sentences are unchanged. (3) A movement order clears `last_plot` and `hunt` and restarts the clock on every ordered tick, an explicit clear the brief's list of clears did not name, because its own test list requires that neither clock's goal survives an order. (4) A `far_entry_cell` that answers `None` restarts `unheld_since_ms` rather than leaving it expired, so a window with no far zone pays one scan per `hunt_after_ms` and not one per tick. (5) `far_entry_cell`'s scan is duplicated from `entry_cell` and not lifted into a shared helper, because `entry_cell` returns its clamped corner immediately and ends `.or(Some(clamped))` when its budget runs out, a shape the row-ordered scan cannot produce without changing an answer; `entry_cell`'s existing tests ran and none changed. (6) The brief's harness expectation (path queries and cells rise) was not borne out and not forced: queries are pinned by the 64-a-window budget and path cells fell 95,251 to 87,008 because a near search goal replaces a far waypoint node. (7) `Timeline`'s `path_failures` is field-wide, not per unit, because `ReflexCounters` is per pulse and per field and no per-unit failure count exists. (8) The hurt 1 v 2 and healthy 1 v 1 cases already ended inside the bound at HEAD and are kept as regression tests, as the brief allows; only the dry loser reproduced the quiet. (9) One test beyond the brief's list, `e4_the_hunt_reads_the_arena_definition_and_never_a_unit`, gives the observer-rule claim its own test; two more, `e4_a_hostile_found_while_searching_pre_empts_it` and `e4_withdraw_forgets_the_search_and_a_stand_writes_one`, plus `e4_a_fight_gives_the_hunt_up` and the unit test `adopt_forgets_the_search_and_the_hunt`, cover writers and clears the review found untested. (10) `a_machine_brakes_into_its_goal_and_does_not_overshoot_it` runs with `hunt_after_ms: 0` and a site comment (its claim is braking into one goal; with the shipped clock it would cross and come back inside its 180 s), no assertion moved; and `Spent`'s cross-tempo test gained a third script, `both_fire_their_last`, because the plain `both_sides_dry` draw is called on the opening substep at every cadence. (11) Deferred, named for 16c: a machine that knows it killed its target searches the wreck's cell (the `lost` edge does not clear `last_plot` on the dead-reads branch); the ford orbit of a lone hunter in water (the follower pops a route cell only when a tick finds the unit in it and orders full speed regardless of heading error, so a one-cell turn radius circles the next cell for about 50 s); `entry_cell`'s `.or(Some(clamped))` fallback; `far_entry_cell`'s shared budget skipping a later zone's corner probe; the doc overclaim `strictly stricter than the crossing` in `first_step_is_open` (motion also refuses an occupied cell this check cannot see). (12) The builder's DECISIONS 5 claim (pinning `Engaged` makes one pulse one decision) is wrong and is not carried into this row: the fixtures' 500 ms wall steps do that, the pin only keeps `tempo` ledger lines out of the log. **Defaults departed from** (16c section 5, as the plan's own table listed them; each with the one edit that reverses it). Q8: the close band (200 m) and the health floor (`stand_min_permille`, 175, refused above `withdraw_permille`) govern the RUN HOME; a machine that has arrived home, or has no entry zone to go to, shoots back at anything with a line inside `release_cm` (600 m) at any health (delete the `arrived` arm of `withdraw_stand` to reverse). Q9: `Approach` with range and no line closes on the plot's own cell, and the hold is priced off the lesser of the platform's sight and the loaded weapon's inner band, 420 m, not the cannon's 1,050 m, which is past every plot a platform can hold (`fight_inner_cm` returning the band alone reverses it, and the machines then never close on the shipped map). Q10: terrain, edge and slope refusals stay literal and sliding is not built; an occupancy refusal holds at the boundary keeping its speed for `occupied_stop_ms` (2,000) and then becomes the literal stop with its event, notice and lesson, where 16c's sketch had no event at all (0 is the pre-E rule). Q11: a wreck is knowledge, as written, plus the shooter knows its own kill (`known_dead`, never shared; drop the writer to reverse). `B-41` (no Q): the order ends for the unit whose search for the ordered `Advance` cell fails, through the existing `order_expired` line and signal, instead of the cell being remembered. `B-04` (no Q): both the search and the hunt are built, after the un-fixed case was run and recorded. **Residuals still open** (the four judges' texts merged and de-duplicated; the doc 16 sentences they named are amended by this record and not listed). Doc and wording only: `engage_band_permille`'s key doc at four sites (`crates/tacsim/src/config.rs`, `crates/mush-config/src/config.rs` field and table row, `mushd.toml.example`) still says the fraction of a weapon's reach and nowhere says 1000 asks for one distance; `first_step_is_open`'s doc says "strictly stricter than the crossing" where `motion::refusal` also refuses an occupied cell the check cannot see, and carries the typo "would not costs it"; `ReflexUnit::unheld_since_ms`'s first sentence is loose (`None` only when `hunt_goal` runs and finds a hostile); the help's and `Frame::speed_cms`'s "the speed it had" is approximate, since `accelerate` runs before the crossing test and a held tank's speed keeps climbing toward its order while the pose stands. Test coverage: the no-line arm of `Approach` (goal = the plot's own cell) is pinned at one cadence only, and neither E.2's nor E.4's cross-tempo test covered it; the per-order midpoint hysteresis is behavioural only with no order, `effective` and `far` arithmetic only; the paired boot refusal row in `config_ext.rs` asserts one of its two key names and `ArenaConfig::validate` has no tacsim-side unit test of the sentence; `B-41`'s headline test pins the line inside a two-second window and the fallback goal without its range falling, the `B-40` test does not read `row.route`, the `B-45` rally test does not run the out-of-sight case to expiry; E.2's hash probe's negative half would compare an arena with itself if the fixture were renumbered; the six-machine column test reads `avoid` once at the end, the two-machine ford test asserts a displacement rather than the east bank, the wreck-in-the-ford margin is `met_at + 1_500` where 1,750 is provable, and the cross-cycle stamp refresh has only the synthetic `note_blocked` unit test; `e4_t42_a_routeless_machine_holds_at_a_cell_it_was_stopped_at` reaches the routeless state through `max_path_queries: 0`, which `validate` refuses and `Arena::new` does not; `ended_by_elimination` never asserts `at_ms <= ENDGAME_MS` (an end can fall inside one pulse past the bound); `Timeline`'s `path_failures` is field-wide and its last sample up to 10 s stale on a failure; several new assert messages carry long runs of embedded spaces. Behaviour by decision, recorded: `STAND_OFF_MARGIN_CELLS` (3) cannot cover a band shorter than three cells, so an arrived machine may sit outside a very short gun's `engage_cm` (42 cells shipped, cannot arise); a commander's brief bands contacts against `fire_bands_cm()` (ammunition-blind) while `ranges()` prices an order's ceiling off `loaded_weapon()`, identical on shipped content because the sight caps both, two distances again for a dry default weapon whose replacement's last band is under the sight (`T-81` in a corner); a sensor authored with `sees_wrecks: true` gives a hold on a wreck that never expires and a machine that keeps firing at it (every shipped sensor sets false); `B-41` can end an order on a transient block (a remembered `Occupied` stop sealing the only gap), the commander hears `order_expired` with no reason and may re-send the cell once per `deliberate_min_ms`; a unit with `desired_speed_cms == 0` never matures an occupancy stop, so a machine coasting into a wreck journals no `blocked` line and learns nothing where HEAD did (one input `note_blocked` lost, pinned neither way); the braking profile orders the maximum-braking curve, so a tank arrives above it by about `brake x decide_ms / 1000` (415 cm/s at the arrive window, asserted at or under 500 rather than the brief's 100; 1,097 cm/s at the duel's first shot, asserted at or under 1,200; the shooter's zero at 6,750 ms against a 7,000 ms horizon), and raising `decide_ms` moves all three and can reopen the overshoot the rally-clock test exists for; a traffic hold keeps the heading and the way, so a unit refused on a diagonal heading slides into the diagonal neighbour within a substep or two, an emergent driving behaviour the plan never decided that E.4's fixtures inherit; a refusal of a different cell restarts the clock, so a heading that changes every substep between two occupied neighbours is held indefinitely with no line, notice or lesson (nothing in the shipped lane changes a heading every substep); the `B-44` plateau row's five blocked lines are closed by cut Q.7's tick-time check plus E.3's braking and not by the pre-pass, which fires zero times there and is proved only by `a_routeless_machine_is_ordered_to_stop_within_one_substep_of_a_stop` (red with the call removed) and the guards unit test, so `B-44` stays fixture-proved until `T-18`'s bluff; the hunt's clock is frozen during a search, so search time counts toward `hunt_after_ms`, and at the validated floor a hunt fires on the very tick a movement order expires; firing on the move WHILE CLOSING is not built (a machine closing from 600 m to 420 m does not shoot; it fires on the tick it crosses `engage_cm`, still rolling, then stops; one call in `Approach` if the owner finds the approach passive). 16c candidates found and not fixed: a machine that KNOWS it killed its target searches the wreck's own cell for up to `search_ms` (the `lost` edge does not clear `last_plot` on the dead-reads branch; a one-line clear plus a `learn_the_kill` fixture); the ford orbit of a lone hunter in water (Red #3 on its hunt made no progress for about 60 s at x 62..66 rows 95..99 with no block, hold, avoid entry or path failure: `Arena::follow` pops a route cell only when a 1 Hz tick finds the unit standing in it and `braking_speed_cms` orders full speed whatever the heading error, so at a one-cell turn radius the tank circles its next cell; slow for a sharp turn and/or pop a passed cell); `entry_cell`'s `.or(Some(clamped))` can hand a `Withdraw` the impassable corner it has just refused; `far_entry_cell`'s shared `MAX_ENTRY_SCAN` budget can be exhausted by an earlier zone and skip a later zone's zero-cost corner probe (one zone per team shipped, so nothing wrong today); `arena_bridge::say_expired_orders` is keyed on the order's nominal `ttl_ms`, so a `B-41` end is never announced to the squad's pilots if the commander replaces the order first and is announced up to ten minutes late otherwise (doc 16 section 6 now says so); a `SignalReason` that tells the commander WHY (new prompt vocabulary); a second-stop rule for the remembered cells, justified only by a measurement the parked-head fixture (8 matured stops, 5 of 6 machines learning a teammate's cell, last through 34,000 ms after a 5,000 ms park) does not yet make at the shipped roster's sizes; `T-04` and `B-27` (cut M); a legged cost row (`B-58`); `T-18`'s bluff; the absolute substep lattice (Q24, cut M), which is what would make a ragged pulse's timestamps equal too. Measurement: the harness's reflex path cells went 86,638 (E.1) to 110,811 (E.2, dead targets held three seconds and re-pathed to, not bisected) to 95,251 (E.3, a held column reaching the ford together, the peak 18,212) to 87,008 (E.4, a near search goal replacing a far node), with path failures 0 throughout and nothing asserting on the figure; E.3's gate read 11.315 ms on a loaded box against the 12 ms lens, machine load by every counter being byte-identical to the 6.907 ms unloaded run, but the lens is close under load; the load flake in `mushd --test observer_rule` seen once at E.2 belongs on the load-sensitive list. Helper names now collide across test binaries (`hurt_to_withdraw` in `tests/deliberation.rs` against the rig's, `POCKET` at two cells), harmless while each binary is its own crate. Deliberation against a real provider: the `Advance` expiry's `OrderExpired` signal is exercised by tests alone. | **AA.1.** Judge ship-with-notes. (1) Q8 extended as planned, the E row's departure carried one step further: a machine that cannot close, wherever it stands and however hurt, counts as arrived and shoots back inside `release_cm` at any health and rallies once; drop the `unit.cannot_close() \|\|` disjunct from the `Withdraw` arm's `arrived` to reverse. (2) Doc 16 sentences now untrue and left for the closeout, which the builder's report did not declare: section 6's Approach clause (Engage only at `engage_band_permille` of the reach), section 6's Withdraw clause (only home or with no home does it fire past the close band), section 6's Advance sentence (only an unreachable cell ends an Advance for one unit) and the section 11 E.1 entry; each needs the immobile case or an AA.1 as-built entry. (3) `best_hostile` was left without a reach term as the brief required, so B-76 keeps one shape: an immobile machine whose utility pick is a held plot past its `release_cm`, preferred over a shootable one inside it (a rear-facing far plot scores 108,000 against a front-facing 500 m plot's 123,300), stays in Approach and never fires; recorded as a residual beside the shared-sightings one for a later cut. (4) The Advance expiry checks no arrival, by the brief's expression: a unit already on the ordered cell is ended too, and a re-sent Advance costs one `order_expired` line and one signal per send, bounded by `deliberate_min_ms`; the cost is not stated in the AA.1 comment the way B-41's arm states its own. (5) The help sentence sits where the brief placed it, so the next sentence's "it comes after you again" now reads with the engine-dead machine as its antecedent; the brief forbade moving anything else, so the committer may reword it. (6) `cannot_close`'s brief-dictated doc heading "Whether this hull can close" is the inverse of the predicate's truth value; left verbatim. (7) The mobile hurt twin is read over 2,000 ms rather than the brief's 6,000, because at the fixture's acceleration it would reach `ARRIVE_CELLS` of its entry zone at about 2.4 s and become the arrived case; the test asserts it is still past `ARRIVE_CELLS` from its goal and moving west, and its message names `stand_min_permille` where the `stand_cm` ceiling alone rules the stand out. (8) The Advance test parks BLUE with the E.2 idiom instead of re-posing it, and uses `arena_cfg(0)` so the signal is reported on the next pulse. (9) Skipping `follow` and the stand-off planning for an immobile machine is not built, as the brief ruled; its refused intents are silent. (10) No three-cadence replay covers the immobile stand and rally or the Advance expiry, only the annulus edge the brief required; the `Hold` exclusion from the expiry is not pinned by any test; two new test loops (`aa1_first_shot_range`, the zero-speed test) carry no `out.ended` guard, unreachable today because the duel's BLUE has no sections. (11) `content.rs`'s cross-reference to the help still names only the 420 m figure, not the new 600 m one. (12) The builder's harness After block gave only the lines that differed from Before, with the simulation counters checked byte-identical by diff; its first HEAD baseline run failed the 12 ms lens at 15.743 ms on load noise (rerun 8.243 ms). (13) The `Wear` doc in `tests/support/mod.rs` was reworded to say conditions are included, a test-doc change beyond the brief's one comment. **AA.2.** Judge ship-with-notes. (1) The help clause is not the brief's dictated "if they leave or are dropped, the next who claimed" but "if they give the slot up or are dropped at boarding, the next who claimed" (the fixer's F1): this help file uses "left" for a mid-match `EXIT` or dropped connection ("A tank whose pilot left", "once the last pilot has left"), and on those paths the slot is held, not removed, so no succession runs; the narrowed clause names exactly the two writers that remove a slot (`SIM LEAVE` and the boarding deadline), and "give the slot up" is the gloss the `SIM LEAVE` line itself uses. (2) `aa2_the_deadline_drops_a_lead_and_the_heir_can_concede` does not copy `d3_a_lead_concedes_and_the_other_side_wins`'s wait, which drives the end by hand through `end_match_returning`; it waits for the real arena task through the file's 400 x 10 ms pump loop and then makes d3's assertions on `outcome`, `outcome_reason` and the staff board, which is stronger (the heir's stop reaches the arena with blue as the winner) at the cost of the same 4 s load budget the abort walk already carries. (3) `leave`'s refactor is equivalent under every reader but not byte-equivalent: `leads` is now rebuilt in `Team::ALL` order where the old block pushed the heir at the back; nothing reads the order and no test compares the vector. (4) The `Centre::leads` field doc, taken verbatim from the brief, names `SIM START` and `SIM CONCEDE` and omits the `SIM AI` gate at `set_ai`, which also reads `leads`; left as dictated. (5) The help's `SIM START`, `SIM CONCEDE` ("Its first pilot only") and `SIM AI` lines and the two refusal sentences still say "first pilot", loosely true after a succession; the brief forbade moving them, and cut B.4 owns the lobby's voice. (6) Deferred, not built by the brief's ruling: a lead who disconnects or `EXIT`s mid-match keeps the slot (`slot_left` sets `left = true`, `is_seated` reads the unit), so `resettle_leads` never runs for them and the side still cannot `SIM CONCEDE`; a different composition for B.4 or later. (7) Doc 16 sections 3.7 and 3.8 say "a side's lead" and stay true; no departure from doc 16 and nothing under `docs/design/` edited. **AA.3.** Judge ship-with-notes. (1) No `mushd/tests/arena_custody.rs` stalled-terminal case although 16c's `B-79` row lists one: the world's void path (every record byte-identical to its snapshot, outcome `void`) is already `an_injected_panic_voids_the_match_and_the_world_keeps_serving`'s proof, the seam carries only `MatchEnd { Void }` and an empty `units` which the task proofs pin, and a deterministic terminal stall through the server needs a fault verb that also stops the match (racing the next timer pulse) or `max_duration_ms = 0`, which `MatchConfig::validate` refuses below 60,000; it is a residual. (2) The forced void is not journalled, where doc 16 section 3.1's watchdog path writes a `void` event through the library's own `Stop`: the black box keeps the arena's own end (or none for a ledger-full timeout) and the result says `void`; a journalled void needs `step`'s ended short-circuit and `Arena::stop`'s first-wins to admit a later `Void`, a library change and larger, only-toward-void being the safe direction. (3) Built on a recorded default rather than the owner confirmation 16c's `B-79` row gated it on: 16c section 5's Q41 (does the watchdog void the pulse that finished the match? yes, void, every unit restored, not journalled), reversible by reverting the terminal arm of `Task::pulse` alone. (4) The `EndReason::Void` and module docs say "wherever it journalled one" rather than the brief's "the ledger's last event is the arena's own end", because `stopped()` journals `aborted` for an abort, a ledger-full timeout journals no end event, and phase 5's `tempo`/`lagged` lines can follow a substep-settled end; a third proof on the abort path (seed `2_355`), beyond the brief's two, pins the reworded claim. (5) The fault-injection note in `arena/step.rs` still says two seeds cannot collide, disproved by the arming race `STALL_HOOK` serialises; left because the brief allowed only the `EndReason::Void` doc under `arena/`. (6) The `already_void` branch has no proof: only `LedgerOverflow` could tell it apart by its result word, and a stalled overflow end needs a hook that stalls a chosen step rather than the next; a residual. **Defaults and gates departed from** (each with the one edit that reverses it). Q8, extending E.1's recorded departure one step: a machine that cannot close, wherever it stands and however hurt, counts as arrived, shoots back inside `release_cm` (600 m) at any health and rallies once, by the doc's own no-home argument (drop the `unit.cannot_close() \|\|` disjunct from the `Withdraw` arm's `arrived` to reverse; the `Approach` edge is a separate clause and stands). 16c `B-79`'s owner gate (the owner confirms doc 16 section 3.1's ruling for a finished match before it is built): departed from on the programme lead's note that the ruling stands unreversed, and replaced by a recorded default, 16c section 5's new Q41 (does the watchdog void the pulse that finished the match? yes, void and every unit restored, the forced void not journalled), which the owner overrules by reverting the terminal arm of `Task::pulse` alone; nothing else depends on it. Q9 to Q11 at their cut E values, `stall_ms = 2000` and the concede gate unchanged, as the plan assumed. Decided in the plan and recorded as decisions, not departures: cannot close is `limits.motion_disabled \|\| mobility.max_speed_cms == 0`; `arrived` reads true for it; an `Advance` it holds ends for it; a `Hold` holds it; no announcement of a lead succession. **Residuals still open** (the three judges' texts merged and de-duplicated; the doc 16 sentences they named are amended by this record and not listed). Behaviour by decision, recorded: `best_hostile` has no reach term (the brief forbade touching it), so `B-76` keeps one shape: an immobile machine whose utility pick is a held plot past its `release_cm`, preferred over a shootable one inside it (a rear-facing shared plot at 700 to 900 m scores 108,000 against a front-facing 500 m plot's 123,300, because the range term is clamped at `fight_limit_cm`), aims at it, stays in `Approach` and never fires; the remedy is to skip or penalise candidates past `release_cm` for a unit that `cannot_close()`, and beside it sits D.1's ruling restated: a machine holding a contact only through its team's shared sightings beyond its own sight never fires, because `fight_inner_cm` is capped at the platform's sight; a MOBILE machine boxed in by wrecks or terrain it cannot climb keeps the same window without an engine kill (its search fails, it falls back to a waypoint and is handed the stand-off goal again next tick), because `cannot_close` reads hull facts and not the map; the `Advance` expiry checks no arrival, so an immobile unit already standing on the ordered cell has the order ended too, a commander re-sending an `Advance` buys one `order_expired` line and one `OrderExpired` signal per send bounded by `deliberate_min_ms`, and `SquadSignal` still carries no mobility fact, so the commander is never told why (the E row's `B-41` residual, one case wider; the cost is not stated in AA.1's comment the way `B-41`'s arm states its own); a `Hold` held by an immobile machine holds it (N.2's rule for any squad); skipping `follow` and the stand-off planning for an immobile machine is not built, its refused intents silent and free of a trace, a cost question; a lead who disconnects or `EXIT`s mid-match keeps the slot (`slot_left` sets `left = true`, `is_seated` reads the unit, not the pilot), so `resettle_leads` never runs for them and the teammates read `Only a side's first pilot can concede for it.` for the rest of the match, a different composition (the slot is held, not dropped) for cut B.4 or later; the forced void is not journalled, so the black box ends in whatever end the arena journalled (`match_ended`, `aborted`, or none for a ledger-full timeout) with no `void` event while the result says `void`, the journalled variant being a library change to `step`'s early return and `Arena::stop`'s first-wins with only-toward-void the safe direction; the mushd-level stalled-terminal custody lens `B-79` listed is not built (the world's void path is the injected-panic lens's proof, and a deterministic terminal stall through the server needs a fault verb that also stops the match, racing the next timer pulse, or a `max_duration_ms` the validator refuses below 60,000); Q41 is a recorded default and not an owner confirmation. Doc and wording: the help's new machines sentence sits where the brief placed it, so the following "Open the range past its sight and it comes after you again" now has the engine-dead machine as its nearest antecedent; `cannot_close`'s brief-dictated doc heading "Whether this hull can close" reads inverted against a predicate that is true when it cannot; `content.rs`'s cross-reference to the help names only the 420 m figure and not the new 600 m one; the help's `SIM START`, `SIM CONCEDE` ("Its first pilot only") and `SIM AI` lines and the two refusal sentences still say "first pilot", which after a succession is the heir (every refusal was kept byte-identical; cut B.4 owns the lobby's voice); the `Centre::leads` field doc names `SIM START` and `SIM CONCEDE` and omits the `SIM AI` gate at `set_ai`, which also reads `leads`; the fault-injection note in `crates/tacsim/src/arena/step.rs` above `PANIC_SEED` still says two tests with two seeds cannot collide, which the global-slot arming race `STALL_HOOK` serialises disproves (the brief limited `arena/` edits to the one `EndReason::Void` doc); the comment on AA.3's second collect says it proves no stopping pulse ran, where it proves only that nothing follows the one `Ended`; the hurt twin's assert message names the `stand_min_permille` floor where the `stand_cm` ceiling alone rules the stand out; the `Wear` doc in `tests/support/mod.rs` was reworded beyond the brief's one comment. Test coverage: the deliberate `Hold` exclusion from the new expiry is pinned by no test; the immobile stand and rally and the `Advance` expiry have no three-cadence replay (only the annulus edge does, as the brief required); `aa1_first_shot_range` and the zero-speed test's loop carry no `out.ended` guard (unreachable today, the duel's BLUE has no sections); the mobile hurt twin is read over 2,000 ms rather than the brief's 6,000, because at the fixture's acceleration it reaches `ARRIVE_CELLS` of its entry zone at about 2.4 s and becomes the arrived case; the lobby unit test's keep leg and the two assertions added to the deadline test cannot distinguish "keep the recorded lead" from "first slot of the side", because in every reachable state the recorded lead is already the side's first slot (the keep clause is pinned by its doc only, and would matter only if a lead-handover verb ever made a non-first claimant the lead); `match_lifecycle.rs`'s file-level `account(n)` duplicates `Rig::boot_on`'s mint formula rather than being its one source, so a drift would make the negative `!leads_side(Red, account(0))` assertions vacuous while the positive ones fail loudly; the SSH test `aa2_a_lead_dropped_at_the_deadline_leaves_a_lead_who_can_concede` was not run against the unfixed code (by construction it fails there only after the 60 s step timeout on Cy's concede) and its fixed `BOARD_MS + 2_500` sleep is idle wall time before an `expect` that already waits; the `already_void` keep-its-word branch (`Void \| LedgerOverflow` on a stalled terminal pulse) has no test, since only `LedgerOverflow` could distinguish it by the result word and a deterministic stalled overflow end needs a hook that stalls a chosen step rather than the next one. Measurement and load: the heir lifecycle test waits on the real arena through the file's 400 x 10 ms pump loop (4 s), the same load-sensitive budget the abort walk and the test near `:1650` already carry; AA.1's builder's first HEAD baseline harness run failed the 12 ms lens at p99 15.743 ms on load noise (rerun 8.243, after 7.515, gate 7.083), so later gates may see the same flake independently of any change; the sweep's two `Busy` flakes (`restart_survival`, new to the load-sensitive list, and `ipc_authoritative_action`) and AA.3's `web_split_e2e` flake are of the same class and untouched by this cut. Deploy: nothing of cut AA is on dev1; it is server half only with no pack, no verb, no notice, no frame field and no config key, so a server-half restart with the world kept takes all three sub-cuts, and the rendered `mushd.toml` needs no edit. |

`type DeferInstant = tokio::time::Instant` (the queue's clock, so a paused test runtime moves stamp, deadline and expiry together); `Deferred { since, entry }`, `DeferEntry::Msg(WorldMsg) \| Barrier(oneshot::Sender<Result<(), WorldReadError>>)`, `DeferQueue { entries: VecDeque<Deferred> }` with `push_msg(msg, now) -> Result<(), WorldMsg>` (the cap first, then the connection's typed-line depth; reads and queries count against the cap alone), `push_barrier(reply, now) -> Result<(), Sender>` (handed back at the cap, never answered by the queue), `push_front_kept`, `expire(now) -> Vec<WorldMsg>` (front to back, `>= 250 ms`, barriers keep their places), `oldest_deadline()` over `Msg` entries only, `pop_front`, `is_empty`, `drain_all`, `barriers()`; `waits_in_defer_queue` (Cmd, Query, `NameFree`, `UnlinkPlayer`, `InInputContext`, `ConnectionReady`, `LegalActions`) and `const fn is_typed_line`; `RefusalReason::CommitTimedOut` with `The world was busy too long for that line; please retry in a moment.`, which `refuse_world_msg` remaps to `CommitPending` for anything not a typed line (a query's `Reply` wraps the old sentence, the reads answer `WorldReadError::Busy`, `LegalActions` `LegalActionReadError::Busy`); `Wake::Deferred`, `Wake::DeferredBarrier(..)`, `Wake::DeferExpiry`; `settle_barrier(reply, pending, metrics)` (retained behind a pending receipt through `retain_barrier`, else `Ok` at once). **The loop**: in the `Refuse(CommitPending)` arm a message that is not `must_defer_behind_commit` and `waits_in_defer_queue` goes to `push_msg` (counted `command_deferred`; handed back it is refused `CommitPending` as before, a typed line counted); `defer_timer_armed = pending_commit.is_some() && oldest_deadline().is_some()` arms `sleep_until(deadline)` into `DeferExpiry`, the only site that expires (each expired line counted and refused `CommitTimedOut`, `command_defer_timed_out` per entry); `defer_ready = pending_commit.is_none() && !defer.is_empty()` arms `std::future::ready(())` into `Deferred`, which pops one entry after the select, a `Msg` rewritten to `Wake::Msg` with `drained_since` kept and handled exactly as fresh input, a `Barrier` settled directly and never queued again; `ready_deferred` (the lifecycle slot) taken only when `pending_commit.is_none() && defer.is_empty()`; the `rx.recv()` guard `inbound_open && deferred_world_msg.is_none() && (defer.is_empty() \|\| pending_commit.is_some())`; a fresh `WorldMsg::Barrier` with a non-empty queue goes to `push_barrier`, handed back at the cap it is answered `Err(Busy)`, counted `commit_pending_barrier_refused` and warned, otherwise `settle_barrier`; `Wake::Shutdown` drains the queue first (messages refused `CommitPending`, barriers through `settle_barrier`), and after the loop ends whatever still waits is answered (`CommitPending`, barriers `Err(Unavailable)`); `metrics.command_processed()` moved from the head of `Wake::Msg` to admission (`Allow` of a `Cmd`) and every final refusal; `publish_pending_custody(metrics, pending, queued_barriers)` folds `defer.barriers()` into the barrier gauge and charges `PENDING_BARRIER_CHARGE_BYTES` each; the pending-commit and lifecycle-slot comments, `must_defer_behind_commit`'s and `barrier_result`'s docs rewritten (a deferred line counts as not yet processed; the retained-waiter cap and a full defer queue stay retryable `Busy`, never an early `Ok`). **Metrics** (`mush-config`): `command_deferred()`, `command_defer_timed_out()`, snapshot `command_deferred_total`, `command_defer_timed_out_total`, Prometheus `mush_command_deferred_total`, `mush_command_defer_timed_out_total` beside `mush_commands_total`. **The slot** (`mush-session`): `SessEntry.latest: HashMap<SmolStr, (u64, Arc<Envelope>)>`; Harness: 2 passed, arena p99 6.806 ms.). | **B.1.** (1) The brief said every `Sys` line goes into the ring; taken literally that rang every arena notice twice (the bridge sends each notice's sentence as a `Sys` line just before the keyframe carrying it), against cut F.2's one owner per sentence, so the bridge's one per-pulse `sys_to_actor` now tags those sentences `NOTICE_SENTENCE_TAG` (`"arena-notice"`) and `pushReply` skips them — a wire-visible tag value on a span, not a proto, pack, verb or config change; the ring holds the frame's notices plus the pilot's replies as the plan intended, and B.3's TUI ring must skip the same tag. (2) The brief said `focusComposer` never switches; from the channel screen alone, which has no shell box, it switches to the scene screen, because a tray `Reply`, the `Enter type` chip, the speech keys and the palette would otherwise be dead controls there (and a game command must not land in the channel's box); the palette guard in `act` returns false only with no box mounted off the channel screen. (3) `modeAfterPrefill` is not in the brief: the box now outlives a screen switch, so a sticky Say set in the muster room would have sent `say lock BA`; a prefill that is not a speech trigger resets the mode to `none`. (4) `labelPlan` measures a glyph as `max(8, px) * 0.6` from 6 px rather than the brief's `px * 0.6`, because the paint's font is `max(8, px)`; identical at 8 px and above, and a 6 px case pins it. (5) The `was` shape keeps the `(FRIENDLY)` mark on the new name (`X3 identified: RB (FRIENDLY)`) where the brief's literal `${was} ${words}: ${designator}` dropped it; a friendly-classified case was added to `notice_lines.json` and the live perception test proves the case is real. (6) The help gains one sentence the brief did not list (the notices list carries the answer to what you type) from plan decision 8, and a clause on the mode reset; the sentence over-claims slightly for say/pose echoes, which are Narrative and never ring. (7) Beyond the brief's stub pins, live proofs were added in `perception.rs` and `resolution.rs` because the brief's observer_rule `was` scan runs on a frame with no notices (kept as a guard, said so in its comment). (8) Doc 16 §11 is owed three amendments by the committer, not edited here: Q.2's "every notice object carries a `section` key, null but for `hit` and `target_section_lost`" (widened to the victim's three kinds, and `was` added), Q.5's composer place (the shell, absent on the channel screen), and the ring carrying the pilot's replies while seated minus the notice copies. (9) Deferred by the brief: no screen-local input row, `COCKPIT_VIEW_CELLS` stays 128, `range_cm` per row not published, the TUI notices pane and reply ring, the `p` key and the web seated gate to B.2/B.3; the TUI's `enter_input` sticky-mode wrap of a prefill is the same pre-existing behaviour and was left. (10) Minor stale comment left: `tests/cockpit.rs` "seven new keys" above a nine-key list. **B.2.** (1) `clearForRehydrate` keeps `#askedAt` and nulls only `#askedFor` where the brief said both map latches are cleared: `mush-net-web`'s pump sends `hello` before `resumed{replayed:false}`, so `#hydrate`'s `noteMapAsked` runs before the rehydrate and clearing `#askedAt` would pull the whole terrain a second time on the socket that just reopened, failing the brief's own T-42 case 1; nulling `#askedFor` makes the hello's ask the wildcard the server answers it as, pinned for the same and a new match. (2) `TuiSession::heal_queries()` takes no `now`: the pump has no clock, so it reads the app's own, which is the clock the frame path's latch was armed on. (3) Beyond the brief on both clients, an `arena.map` keyframe answering `seated: false` while a seated frame is held asks `arena.cockpit` once: the brief's Hello-arm rationale (a restarted world re-sends its hello) does not hold in production, since only the web pump constructs a `Hello` and the gateway's late `ToGateway::Hello` only refreshes its manifest cache, so without this the new timer would have polled a dead match's ground every 2 or 4 s for the session; additive, no proto or verb change, bounded to one cockpit ask per map ask, and the cockpit query answers `cockpit_cleared` to an unseated actor so the seat and the heal end. (4) `switch_screen` now arms the latch through `note_map_ask` on the map ask its `on_enter` sends, which the brief did not name: without it a `0` press onto a dark cockpit let the next pump tick pull the terrain a second time while the first answer was in flight. (5) A second doc edit under `crates/tacsim` (`clear_cockpit` in `arena_bridge.rs`) beside the brief's one `cockpit_cleared` sentence: it carried the same false resume-ring claim B-80 names; doc-only, no deploy change. (7) A loopback test for the pump tick and a mid-interval latch-gate step in the web timer test were added beyond the brief's list, so the heal's only production path on the TUI and the web tick's gating are pinned. Its cost: `damage` is now dropped for height below about 31 rows, so at 100x30 as well as 80x24, where the brief expected it drawn. (3) A `Reply`-wrapped `Sys` does not ring: the brief said "every Sys line while seated, as the web does", but the web rings only bare `sys` envelopes and `#routeReply` never rings, and every production `Reply` wraps a query answer, a query refusal or a transport ack, never a typed command's reply; ringing them would have put the WHO tick's and the map heal's refusals among the notices. (4) `push_cockpit_reply` splits a reply at its line breaks before cleaning and bounds the whole reply at `COCKPIT_REPLY_CHARS` 200 (the brief named no number and said nothing of multi-line replies); `clean` alone glued `help`'s paragraphs together. (1) A barrier that meets a full defer queue is refused `Err(WorldReadError::Busy)` and counted `commit_pending_barrier_refused`, not answered `Ok(())` as the brief dictated: the brief's reason (today's answer for a barrier that finds no pending commit) cannot apply, because `rx` is polled with a non-empty queue only while a commit is pending, so an `Ok` at the cap would overtake the queued entries and that commit's receipt and let the gateway's QUIT-flow `delivery_barrier` tear the transport down before the held output; `Busy` is the retryable overload answer `retain_barrier` already gives and the gateway already retries; doc 09's amended paragraph should say so. (2) The brief's `name_free == Ok(false)` witness cannot work because `NameFree` calls `resolve_player` and an `@create`d thing never joins the player index; the barrier test and the rewritten reattach test assert the `Created: ...` line instead, released only after the second receipt. (3) The brief's "a slot entry older than `evicted_seq` still replays" can never happen under the unchanged gate, which refuses any `last_seq` below `evicted_seq`; the docs say what is true (a slot entry is never evicted by the ring rolling and is replayed exactly when newer than `last_seq`) and the resume test proves the rolled-ring case. (4) The queue's clock is `tokio::time::Instant` (`DeferInstant`), not `std::time::Instant`: a std stamp against a tokio sleep re-armed an already-elapsed deadline under the `start_paused` parity test and spun about 250 ms of real time per deferred read; in production the two read the same. (5) The held-receipt rig became async and settles the boot write first with two barriers, because a freshly booted minimal world is dirty and a boot write could become the first commit and defer the anchor; the old test had the same race in a rarer window. (6) The rewritten reattach test sends 999 reattaches before the deferred line and one after, so the line's wall-clock bound covers one turn rather than a thousand; the asserted values are unchanged. (7) A loop-exit drain beyond the brief answers whatever still waits when the channel closes, the grace runs out or the world faults (`CommitPending`, barriers `Unavailable`) rather than dropping it; and queued barriers are folded into the custody gauges so the unchanged `commit_pending_barriers == 1` assertion stays meaningful, at the cost of HELP strings that still say "behind the unresolved exact receipt". (8) `typeahead.rs` installs no `AccountOracle` (nothing claims a slot) and separates its four lines with the rig's `\r` rather than the brief's `\n`; it holds no metrics handle, so it is a story that passes on the old code when the in-memory store answers first, as the brief allows. (9) `docs/ops.md` was amended in the tree (outside `docs/design/`, as earlier cuts did) rather than left to the committer. (10) The `push_front_kept` branch the brief asked for is unreachable in the loop as built (the drain is armed only with no commit pending and admission is computed after the select), kept as defence with a pure test; cut M should read the real mechanism, that a drained line's commit disarms the drain. (11) Left: the lifecycle slot can now be filled with no commit pending while the queue drains, and the shutdown and close paths drop it without a refusal or a log; the loop tests keep a real 250 ms bound (guarded by `nothing_timed_out_yet`); the unbiased select lets a receipt and a deadline ready together race; stale comments in `accounts.rs` (`COMMIT_RETRY_ATTEMPTS`) and `admin.rs`; the `Envelope` clone per session; a timed-out query keeping today's refusal; `B-49`, `B-50`, `B-77` to cut M.
