(3) B.5's loop: the lifecycle slot can now hold a `ConnUp`, `ConnDown`, `SessionGone` or `Apply` with no commit pending while the queue drains, and the `Shutdown` and `Closed` pending-None branches and the loop-exit drain touch only the queue, so that one message is dropped at shutdown with no refusal and no log (the fix is one `deferred_world_msg.take()` refusal in the loop-exit drain); the `push_front_kept` re-queue branch is unreachable (the drain arm is armed only while no commit pends and admission is computed after the select), so its comment, its surface line and the pure test describe a path that never runs, and cut M should read the real mechanism (a drained line's commit disarms the drain, the entries behind it keeping their `since`); the select is unbiased, so a receipt and the oldest deadline ready on one turn may refuse a line `CommitTimedOut` on the turn its commit resolved (within the design, not pinned); the nine held-receipt loop tests measure the 250 ms bound on the real clock, guarded by `nothing_timed_out_yet()`, and belong on the load-sensitive list; `typeahead.rs` sends its four lines with the rig's `\r`, holds no metrics handle and asserts nothing about the queue, so it passes on the old code whenever the in-memory store answers first, and its module doc contradicts itself on that; the custody gauges `commit_pending_barriers` and `commit_pending_operational_bytes` now count queued barriers (up to 64 beyond the 256 retained cap) while the snapshot doc and the two Prometheus HELP strings still say `held behind the unresolved exact receipt`, so an alert keyed on 256 misreads a drain; a timed-out query or read keeps today's `Busy`; the per-session `Envelope` clone stays (T-31's other half, and its `only the web bridge can replay` clause is neither fixed nor named); `B-49`, `B-50` and `B-77` go to cut M on this queue's shape. (4) Restart seams: a pilot whose ground IS held when the world restarts behind the gateway keeps the stale seated frame (no timer runs, so nothing asks), which needs a restart signal to the session (cut M's seam); `Hello.panels` and `SessionInfo.character` are recorded only by `render_line_event`, so a world restart while in `@tui` leaves the next TUI's manifest stale until another `Hello`; the web heal interval keeps ticking through `reconnecting` (each tick's `#req` is null on a closed socket and the hello pass re-asks) and the web `p` arm latches even when the socket was not open (at most one 2 s delay). (5) The room memory: `remember_the_room` records every `Room` and `Reply { Room }`, so a line-mode `look #123` on a remote room, or the pod interior `BOARD` now shows, is the room the next `@tui` opens on, with that room's exit keys (the TUI's own `Room` arm already had this semantics). (6) Test gaps: `applyMap` keeps a held map on the unseated answer with no test; the `notice_lines.json` guard asserts kinds plus the one friendly line, so trimming other shapes stays green on both clients; the jsdom mount has no DOM-level observer assertion (the pure-function check stands); `on_frames`' different-`match_id` reset is untested (`Rig::push_frame` takes the centre's id); the new `BOARD`, `EXIT` and ejection room views are not swept by `names_nothing` (`sim_solo` moved its window past the `EXIT` view because the team room's own `#id` tripped the digit check); observer block (e) reads `VIEW` with `ask` and the bystander's second-connection attach runs `names_nothing` with no sentinel; the observer-rule re-open check relies on one connection's replies arriving in order; `honour_min_heights` is a shared rule for every screen at sizes no golden covers; the pump-tick loopback test waits on the real 4 s WHO tick; `svelteTesting()`'s autoCleanup pushes its hooks into all 43 node-environment files (`autoCleanup: false` plus a local `afterEach(cleanup)` would confine it); jsdom 30 needs node `^22.22.2 \|\| ^24.15.0 \|\| >=26`, which CI's `node-version: "22"` satisfies today. `match_::abort` answers `AbortOutcome { Stopping, Finished, Busy }` and on `Busy` changes nothing, `SIM ABORT` and `SIM CONCEDE` read `The arena is busy; try that again in a moment.`, `@arena/abort` reads `Match {id} is busy; (2) `B-50` test 4a puts the `Released { RED_PILOT }` first at epoch 2 under `Live`s 3 to 19 rather than among or above them, so HEAD's drop-oldest buffer would drop exactly the release and the proof discriminates, and the standing `Live` takes the reflex hand-over back in the same phase 1 so the one journalled `startup` is the seat's; 4b's `BLUE_PILOT` half is trivially met because `apply_control(Live)` never adds a pilot to the roster. (9) `T-82`'s doc-16 wording is not yet made true: doc 16 section 3.1 phase 2 and section 11 still say discrete actions are queued per unit bounded at `intents.max_pending_per_unit`, and the committer must reword them to a seat's `max_pending_per_unit` discrete orders per unit per arena second with the machine's orders uncounted, the `pending` queue unbounded, the task's per-unit pre-pulse bound at that value plus one and the inbox sized `max_pending_per_unit × units + 3·units + 2` with the reserve closed to intents, beside moving the 16b section 9 constant row; the rate also holds per arena second only while pulses are at most a second apart, which the shipped sensor pass guarantees. (10) The rate is counted on `UnitState`, so every seat of one tank shares one count until multi-station lands. (11) `weapons.rs`'s `replay` driver now steps each due beat in its own zero-dt pulse; no earlier script had two beats at one time, so every earlier replay is unchanged. (12) The once-a-match warn for held-notice drops is not asserted, `tacsim` having no tracing subscriber in its tests. **M.2.** (1) `B-51`'s cross-tempo beam test runs on a pulse-end driver (the accepting pulse 4,500→5,000 at every cadence, because M.1's seat-order rate counts on the pulse's start second and is hashed), not the brief's zero-dt driver: under the zero-dt driver a beat's pulse runs no substep, so the brief's own `ran > 0` guard never drains there and "one more than its partition" cannot hold; the guard was kept as the rule, the drain comment says the zero-dt replays do not prove the drain, and a pilot's beam in a zero-dt replay still resolves in the next pulse's first substep at every cadence. (2) `LossSettles` is settled in the staging scan at the position it popped, not inside the due arm as the brief wrote it, because a same-substep re-detection's `detected` reaches the cancel arm only in the scan and settling in the loop would say a loss already cancelled; this also keeps every `held_losses` write after the substep's hash. (3) A held loss whose window closes at or before the end of its own substep (only `pass_ms` shorter than the substep, config floor 50 against 250) gets no due event and is settled by the scan that staged it (the M.2 review's `M2-R4`): scheduling it into the past made the drain run a substep at one cadence and not another. (4) Phase 5 journals no `tempo` or `lagged` line on any pulse a substep ended, eliminations included, which the brief did not ask for but its "the timeout is the last line" assertion requires under cut W.2's rule. (5) The `B-74` arm is narrower than the brief: only a `!numbered` and `Classified` row at staging, so an own-pass re-opened row a share classified in the same substep is said once by the share's own line and a row already gone is not announced. (6) The stopping-distance look-ahead in `hold_routeless` is beyond M.2's timestamp items: 16c `B-44`'s E.3 closure was a window artifact that T-02 exposed, and the fix landed here rather than a test change, so 16c should mark `B-44` reopened and re-closed. (7) Doc 16 is amended by the committer, not only §11 (T-03) and §3.1 (T-05) as planned but also §3.1's phase list (the duration bound and the hysteresis expiry as due events, the phase-4 drain, phase 5 silent after an end, motion integrating to the substep end past the deadline) and §3.6's hysteresis sentence (a cancel whose row came back renamed is said as a regression when `unknown` and as an identification when a mesh insert arrived classified). (8) Four existing fixtures were re-anchored for T-02's one-tick-earlier machine startup with their claims kept: the `e4_field` start cell (2, 8)→(1, 8), the deliberation pin applied before the run, the cold-tank window 1,500→1,000 and the dry-withdrawal floor 4,500→4,000 (the run makes 4,490; the magazine-difference claim unchanged). (9) An elimination in the deadline's substep keeps `settle_elimination`'s substep-end stamp and the deadline substep integrates motion to its end, both left as they were and pinned by tests. (10) The brief's due-heap counters are not reported by the harness. **M.3.** (1) The new-edge condition for ground, slope and the window's edge is 'a cell not Chebyshev-adjacent to the held one' (`same_stop`), not the brief's 'a different cell': keyed on the exact cell, a slanted push at a west bank or the west window edge alternates between the diagonal and the orthogonal neighbour once the clamp slides the tank to its cell's corner (measured 101 lines in 61.8 s), so a turn that pushes the same bank round an L's corner inside one cell is one stop; the cost is that a 90° turn from an east bank onto a north bank in the same cell is silent, which is what HEAD did too, and `Occupied` stays exact so a second hull is still a new edge and still reaches the avoid list. (2) The clear-on-move-away direction is cell-to-cell (`held minus here` in whole cells) rather than pose-to-held-centre in centimetres, because the brief's formula measured 3 lines over two pulses and 14 over thirteen on the ragged driver (a slide past the centre line reads as away). (3) For `Edge` the direction is the window's outward normal on the axes whose pre-move pose sits on the window's first or last centimetre, not on the cell's row and column, because the cell-derived normal was diagonal in a corner cell and re-armed every pulse; `blocked_at` for an edge stop is `nearest_on_window` of the moved pose and can name the neighbour along the edge, which the `Held` and `moved_away` docs now say. (4) `plan_touches_bands` is a `pub(crate)` associated fn on `Arena` (it must be callable from `damage.rs` and `heat.rs`) narrowed from 'any Section cell' to Section cells named `internals`, with `AdjustCell` counted, exact against `Condition::of`. (5) The failing-first driver is 1,030 ms at 120° rather than the brief's 1,000 ms at 30° off, which passed at HEAD; the cross-tempo beat is 60° (north of east) because the tank met the band 13 cm into its row and a south creep would legitimately be refused a second cell. (6) The T-23 test proves 'a quiet pulse takes no hash' through a `cfg(test)` thread-local counter in `state_hash`, a test-only global under `arena/`, because the slot reads `Outside` after any pulse. (7) The X.6 substep-length test no longer prints `harness_x6_substep_blocked` (nothing read it) and asserts `[1, 1]`. (8) The harness `held_at_end` counts `unit.held.is_some()` rather than the per-substep flag (29 either way). (9) The phase-4 hash value moved from phase start to the state at the run's first journalled line. (10) Doc 16 edits owed to the committer: §4 'draw' becomes 'draws', the `blocked` line's `cause`, `hit.armor_section`, the result record's `seed` with its staff-only note, and the T-04 rule text as built (adjacency, cell-to-cell direction, edge normal on boundary axes); the 16c rows for these nine; the seed-derived contact id on the wire (plan M-04) stays open. (11) Deferred: the -1 cm tail-floor asymmetry itself (a 16c note), a ragged-cadence cross-tempo witness, a refused-miss two-draw assertion, a step-driven machine pin past the TTL. **M.4.** (1) `step_refusal` is `#[cfg(test)] pub(crate)` rather than plain `pub(crate)`: its only caller is the path.rs property, an uncalled crate-visible function fails `-D warnings` under the dead-code lint, and an `#[allow(dead_code)]` would move the restriction-lints census; it builds the crossing through the same `Crossing::between` that `advance` calls, so the property still asks about the crossing motion builds. (2) The biped is not re-versioned, departing from the programme lead's note as the brief anticipated: its legs have authored `disable_motion_when_all_of` since X.2, so a version 2 would be a digest moved for no content change. (3) `resolve_seen` uses the same past-the-last-band gate as `resolve_shot` (the brief named only `resolve_shot`): otherwise the cockpit would quote 1 per cent beside `beyond` for a shot that resolves at 0; the help now says 0 per cent. (4) `opening_pose`'s zone arm also requires `has_way_out`, beyond the brief's world-pose arm: the zone walk could otherwise still place a unit on a walled-in cell and the scope line would be false; nothing pinned on the shipped ground changed. (5) The perception cull is floored at 1000 per mille inside the library as well as refused at boot, so the cull-not-shorter-than-the-range-test property holds for a hand-built config too. (6) `LINE_TANK_DIGEST` in extensions.rs, a pin the brief did not list, moved with the content (the stock tank's definition digest); its doc records the old value and the boot refit. (7) `Arena::max_speed_cms` keeps the brief's `fastest × 100 / min(100, min_move_cost)` although the order clamp in `apply_intent` keeps every unit at or under its authored speed, so the scale is a conservative margin and no unit reaches the scaled cap today; the docs in state.rs, clock.rs and motion.rs say so after the review, and dropping the scale is left to the lead (doc 16 section 3.1's divisor wording is amended by the committer). (8) A bandless weapon, possible only in a fixture since the loader refuses one, resolves blind at every range while `WeaponSummary::bands_cm` reads it as one band; `band_of` is the one gate the brief chose. (9) `B-53`/`B-54` tests sit in `tests/extensions.rs` against the shipped ECM tank from the catalog rather than in `perception.rs`, the real pod being the stronger fixture; three hand-mirrored fixture profiles (`test_support::line_tank_profile`, `tests/resolution.rs`, the harness) gained the pair rule to match version 2. (10) The pack note sentence is shorter than first drafted, notes being capped at 1024 bytes. (11) Doc 16 sections 3.1, 3.3, 3.4 and 3.5 and 16b section 9's `sensors.max_signature_factor` row and cut-G text are amended by the committer, the builder being barred from docs/design. (12) Deferred, as the plan scoped: `T-18`'s bluff, the Q24 substep lattice, the check-out-only pod and filter condition, and `CellOccupied`/`PoseOffWindow` still naming a unit to the centre (M-12). **Residuals still open after cut M**, merged from the four judges' texts and the plan's own list. (1) The seam. An `Ended` the world drops at its staging queue (`world_task.rs`, `ArenaTasks::stage` refusing at `MAX_STAGED` with a wedged handler) leaves the task closed-and-reported, so `SIM ABORT`, `SIM CONCEDE`, `@arena/abort` and the unattended stop answer `Busy` for the rest of the boot where they once finished the match from snapshots; it needs a wedged handler and 4,096 staged messages, and only a restart clears it. `forward_intent`'s full-inbox answer still reads `is_live()` alone and never `has_reported`. `shipped_digest` is folded at `take_chunks` inside `Arena::step`, before `next_due_ms` and `take_deliberation_signals`: a panic in either after a chunk closed, or `queue_chunk`'s drop-oldest past `max_unflushed_chunks + 1` while the world is not draining (the ledger-overflow void path), makes the reported digest cover bytes the log never received; and the panic arm's open chunk (up to 16 KiB before a fault) still never leaves, the reported digest being over what did. The journalled forced void (AA.3's) is not taken: it needs the library's ended short-circuit and first-wins stop to admit a later `Void`, only toward void being the safe direction. The seat rate is stored on `UnitState`, so every seat of one tank shares one count and `buffer_discrete` is per unit, not per seat (16c `T-54`'s multi-station tank would spend the gunner's budget on the driver's burst); the rate is per arena second only while pulses are at most a second apart, which the shipped 1,000 ms sensor pass guarantees; `PrePulse::take` drains standing controls in `(ObjId, Channel)` key order, not arrival order, which differs only across channels of one unit and is harmless while one station grants all three. A pilot released while frames are held loses every notice held for them, the observer rule's price. Untested: the once-per-match held-notice-drop warn (no tracing subscriber in tacsim's tests), `IntentKind::Order` coalescing, `T-12`'s replace arm (unreachable at HEAD; its proof is A1's seed re-derivation), `@arena/abort` against a running match through the gateway (the pre-crash abort was removed, so the verb's running-match reply is covered in-process only), and `arena_story_split.rs` carries no digest equality because it reads no result document. (2) Timestamps. Motion, heat dissipation and the `blocked` notices still integrate to the substep end past the deadline, only due events being cut at it: a `blocked` stamped after the deadline is journalled just before `match_ended { timeout }` at the deadline, and the returned poses and heat include up to one substep after the whistle under the scripted drivers (in production the task wakes at the deadline, so the overrun is timer slop; pinned by `now_ms == 22_750` against `ended.t_ms == 22_600`). An elimination or `Spent` settled in the deadline's substep carries the substep end as `ended.t_ms` (pre-existing; pinned at 3,250 after a 3,100 deadline). A window shorter than the substep (`pass_ms` 50 to 249, no shipped config) is settled by the scan that staged it, so a mesh share landing exactly on such a deadline cancels where the due-event path says the loss first: deterministic and cadence-independent, but a second one-millisecond rule. `way_ahead_is_open` skips the first cell the walk enters and samples at half a cell, so a line clipping a corner tests the diagonal edge and not the clipped cell, and the look-ahead changed routeless driving field-wide (path cells up 27 per cent and peak path cells up 84 per cent against M.1, attributed to `T-02`'s earlier startup and not fully explained). The zero-dt scripted drivers never exercise the phase-4 drain (a beat's pulse runs no substep), so a pilot's beam in a zero-dt replay still resolves in the next pulse; the drain is proved on the pulse-end driver only. Three behaviours ship without a direct test: a zero-length substep skipping `hold_routeless` and motion, phase 5 journalling no `tempo` or `lagged` line after an elimination, and a wreck's `LossSettles` kept by `destroy_unit`; the held-loss unit test drives `settle_held_loss` by hand and proves the stale-deadline guard, the cancel and the interleave being proved by the perception three-cadence tests alone. The harness reports no due-heap depth, so a leak of `LossSettles` has no regression signal. (3) The hash and the record. `same_stop` keys ground, slope and edge refusals on Chebyshev adjacency, so a 90° turn from an east bank onto a north bank inside the same cell is silent (HEAD's behaviour too; the pilot still reads `HELD`). The `B-27` re-arm is guarded at one ragged cadence only (1,030 ms, 120°, plus the three slanted cases); the cross-tempo beat at 5,000 / 1,000 / 500 ms leaves no tail and would not catch a re-arm through another rounding path. The -1 cm tail floor itself is unchanged: `axis()`'s Euclidean division makes a tank take an asymmetric one-centimetre in-cell step south or west in a pulse tail, and M.3 only stops it re-arming `held` (worth a 16c note). Untested shapes: a `miss{refused}` written after the facet draw carrying two draws, a dispersion miss at a band with no facet table carrying one, the occupied cell reaching a machine's avoid list on a second edge by a step-driven machine (the corner test drives a pilot unit), and a step-driven pin of the tick's avoid refresh past 30 s (proved with `held` hand-set); the hash test's letting-go block compares `None` with `None`. The phase-4 hash value is now the state at the run's first journalled line rather than at phase start (every sequence moves anyway). `hash::STATE_HASHES_TAKEN`, a `cfg(test)` thread-local, is the first test-only global under `arena/`. The harness `blocked_split` buckets are still inferred from the window and not read off the line's own `cause`. The frame's wire key stays `"blocked"` under the Rust field `held` until a client cut renames it on both panels. **The seed-derived contact id on the wire** (plan review M-04, pre-existing): the keyframe's row `id` and a notice's `contact` are the `ContactId` drawn from the seed, and both stages are invertible, so one captured keyframe lets a client recover the keyed state, every contact's object id and every draw of the match; the fix is a per-pilot opaque row key on the cockpit seam (the designator, or a keyed hash under a process secret that never enters the ledger), a client-touching cut because both panels key rows on `id`; until then the seed on the result document is staff-only, no player verb prints it, and 16c `N2`'s report verbs must not. (4) Weapons, sight and entry. `perception.rs`'s two doc sites (the ones 16c `T-26` cites) still say a factor above 1 would lengthen a sensor's range, where the knob now only sizes the cull and 1.0 is its floor and exact value. The `MotionCarry` sign-byte doc says the bytes reset with the carry on every return that zeroes it, but the arc-closing step in `turn` and the target-reached step in `accelerate` leave the byte set until the next substep's early return clears it (harmless and identical across cadences; a reader reconstructing the hash from the doc would be one substep off). The `speed_rate` reset on `advance`'s refusal path and in `stop_at_boundary` is exercised by no test; no three-cadence run reaches the both-closed branch of `turn`, a carry flip or a `beyond` shot (the fixture rates are whole at 250 ms, so no carry is ever banked); `speed_rate` keys on which rate is used and not on the sign of the change, so a carry banked speeding up toward +1,000 survives a reversal order to -1,000 while the target's magnitude still exceeds the current's (under 1 cm/s, deterministic, the brief's own definition). `docs/help/cockpit.md`'s new sentence "the second line ends the tank cannot move" sits below a four-line example whose second line is a hull hit. `path.rs`'s head says every step of a found route is one `step_refusal` accepts, which holds for ground and slope only, `refusal` also answering `Occupied` from the live index the search does not read. A pod's or filter's condition is read at check-out only, so a pod hit to zero mid-match keeps jamming until the hull is a wreck. `CellOccupied` and `PoseOffWindow` still name a unit and a cell in a sentence the lobby announces to the centre (M-12; the three new sentences do not). A bandless weapon, fixture-only since the loader refuses one, resolves blind at every range while `WeaponSummary::bands_cm` reads it as one band. The harness prints `cut_a_p99_ms=5` beside an engaged p99 above it; that constant is reported only and the asserted lens is 12 ms. One shutdown race in `mush-server` `world_task::tests::what_still_waits_when_the_loop_ends_is_answered_not_dropped` on the M.4 builder's run, green on four reruns and in the sweep, not on the load-sensitive list. The biped stays at version 1 by design (its legs carry the pair rule since X.2). Deferred by the plan's scope: `T-18`'s bluff, the absolute substep lattice (Q24), the per-pulse `Frame::same_as` age column (`T-30`, cut U). (5) This ledger's historical rows are not rewritten, by the decision the Q closeout recorded: the cut-G row still says `max_signature_factor` 1.5 mapped to per-mille 1500 and the D.2 row still calls the order bound a per-pulse queue; the constants table above and doc 16 carry what is true now. |

(5) Ten assertion changes beyond the brief's allowed list, all pinning `id % cadence` or first-wake timing, each computed off `stagger_ms` with a precondition asserted: the `state.rs` opening test, `step.rs`'s position-summary test, `deliberation.rs` `e2_b41`'s expiry order, five more `tests/perception.rs` tests, three more `tests/reflex.rs` tests (`m2_a_machines_startup_and_shot` runs one more pulse so the impact lands; `m2_a_weapon_ready_between` puts ready at the midpoint), and the D.1 shipped-config gap bound, which went from 1,050 to a value derived from the fixture (1,250 here) capped at `2,000 − substep` and required to equal the widest gap. `RANGE <spot>` and `BEARING <spot>` are a second resolution arm behind the designator arm, the whole name or its letter case-folded, `Range to The Ford (A): 430 m.` and `Bearing to The Ford (A): 087° (rel 357°).` with no stale clause, `SCAN` and `LOCK` untouched, and a word that is neither keeps the one byte-identical `No such contact.`; X3  A The Ford`; the fixture scene gains three spots (over water, under a contact, off the window) and the four fixture files move once with the TUI goldens, the resume test replays the new payload unchanged, and the observer test gains the map's `spots` and the pre-detection spot battery. **V.2, the result reader.** `Arena::callsigns()` fills `MatchRecord.callsigns` through `open_match`; `UnitResult.callsign`, each pilot row's callsign and `ledger.chunks` reach the result document (`record_version` stays 1; a restart abort writes empty ones); `HistoryRow::from_document` reads a result document into a `MatchBook.history` ring of `HISTORY_DEPTH` rows, loaded before the boot's restart writes and written on every result write from the very value pushed; `civil_utc`, `duration_words`, a closed `outcome_words` table (`blue won (red eliminated)`, `draw (both sides out of ammunition)`, `timed out (every pilot had left)`, `aborted (the server restarted)`, …) and `end_words` beside it are pure and pinned; `SIM HISTORY` prints `#12  blue won (red eliminated)  4 m 32 s  2 v 2  2026-09-24 14:02 UTC  (you flew RA)` newest first, the suffix only with a callsign, and names no unit, pilot, position or contact. **V.3, the debrief.** `match_::report::Fold` is a pure projection from its first commit: for a pilot it keeps only what their own platform held, named and told apart from the tape's `detected`, `classified` and `lost` lines, prints their own shots with their outcomes resolved when the round landed (`cannon at BA: hit, turret.`, a section word only on a target then classified, `a contact` otherwise, no range and no damage), what their rounds destroyed, the hits on their own tank in their own `Struck` vocabulary without the shooter, their own power and locks, their end and the match's end as tail lines never truncated, the timeline sorted by stamp and bounded at 200 while the match runs, and nothing of any other unit's ammunition, movement, detections or positions, no draw, seed, contact id, object number or hash; the folds are built at check-out with the match's own rules, fed in `on_chunk` (each line parsed once) and finished in `finish` into one report document per pilot named on the result's `reports`; `SIM REPORT [<match>]` refuses in one sentence each, in order, a running match, a forgotten one, a restart, a match not theirs and a pre-debrief document, then reads the one document (`still being written; ask again` when the read outran the commit) and says it in blocks of 25 lines so no report can overflow a 256-event connection queue; `@arena/report <match> [<chunk>]` is the staff whole, one page of sixteen chunks a request with `Next:` on its tail, no live, restart or named gate; staff read one line an event with every field, the draws and the seed; the observer test grows a report column that checks each pilot's report against the store's tape for every callsign their platform never classified, the shooting test's target reads no shot, the undetected test's pilot reads no callsign but their own, the bystander is teleported into the lobby before being told they did not fly, and A1 ends by reading the kill. No state slot, namespace, pack, digest pin, draw label, notice kind, wire key or panic-budget move; nothing per pulse; harness delta unchanged. | **The loop**, once per sub-cut and strictly serial, the cut Q loop again: a builder working from a written brief cut from the revised plan (`scratchpad/cuts/V/plan.md`, its dispositions table of eighteen adopted and two rejected findings included) and from the judge's surface text of the sub-cut before; three independent lenses on the uncommitted diff (conformance to the brief and doc 16, correctness, tests); a fixer who fixed or rebutted every finding by id; an independent gate that owned cargo, edited nothing and was allowed one rerun for a load flake; a judge who read the whole diff against the brief and this row and wrote the landed, deviations, residual and surface texts; and an agent commit and push only on a green gate AND a ship verdict. (13) The renderers' copies of the three caps are not pinned to the arena's constants. **Residuals still open** (the four judges' lists, the plan's and the closeout's, merged and de-duplicated; the doc 16, 16b and 16c items every judge owed to the committer are closed by this row and the amendments made beside it). *`ORDER` and the span.* (1) One `ORDER`'s per-unit offers can straddle a pulse in the arena task and journal `ordered` at two timestamps: the debrief would print the order twice and the harness's `orders` count (distinct issuer and `t_ms` pairs, declared an approximation in both directions in `orders_on_tape`'s doc) would read two, while two `ORDER`s from one issuer between the same two pulses read one; neither happens on the harness lanes (the cost run's 55 equals its `issued`), and the exact count needs `issued_ms` on the `ordered` line, which moves the ledger event set. (2) A non-lead's wingman order writes the per-squad `OrderWatch` with their tank as issuer, so it shields the whole squad from the LLM seam and overwrites the lead's watch on that squad (Q43's latest-wins default, nobody told); the span and the expiry sentence read that watch, so a per-unit watch or an issuer set may be wanted; and the lead's and a non-lead's orders on one wingman are latest-wins per unit with no word to either. (3) The span holds for the full ten-minute TTL: the world never learns an early arena end of an order or that a squad is all wrecks, and nothing hands a squad back (Q44, cut Z's `RELEASE` arm); `MIN_UNITS_TO_SPLIT` keeps a four-pod side to one squad, so the span never binds on the shipped centre until cut Z, no test tells one stamp per `ORDER ALL` from one per squad reached, and `span_max` is one end-of-run reading of `Arena::live_orders_by_issuer`, not a peak (the `sample` hook of `run_wakes` is the place to track it when the object is promoted). (4) A tank abandoned by `EXIT` joins no squad and takes no order (squads are fixed at check-out); the `mind link dropped` sentence is said for a human order's expiry in the LLM's words; `Order.spot` names a cell the arena never checks against the spot list (the world wrote both from one `KeySpot`). (5) An `ORDER` the arena cannot apply (`NotReflex` on a tank a human has since boarded, or a moved epoch) is dropped silently by `phase_two_tells`, so the pilot reads the acknowledgement and nothing after, where the help's Refusals section says an arena-refused order is said back; and when every offer fails the cockpit says `Busy` even when the cause was `Gone` or `NoMatch` (as the brief prescribed). (6) `on_deliberation_reply` re-stamps `issued_ms` but forwards the reply's `issuer` and `spot` unchanged (today always `None` from `order_of`; a forged reply on the internal channel is the only route; a one-line reset beside the stamp closes it). (7) `order_ack` keeps the singular `it` for several squads; `RateFull` reads `1 orders` at the legal `orders_per_minute = 1`; the rate window keeps a stamp exactly 60,000 ms old, one second looser than the help's `until the minute has passed`; `CommandConfig::default`'s comment credits the 4 to W8 where it is 16c N3's default. (8) Nothing fails if the `[tacsim.command]` block is removed from `mushd.toml.example` or the instance template (the sibling blocks' convention); `fn order` passes `validate(window)` with `None` when `arena_of` misses (unreachable on a running match); `ArenaRegistry::order_issuer` is public surface beyond the brief; the cost lane's `wall_s` now includes `tape.absorb` (report-only). (9) `authorise_seat` pins only the `NotFocused` arm and the accept path (`NoMatch`, `NoStation`, `Channel`, `LeaseUnknown`, `LeaseHeld` unpinned); hash-before-write is pinned only indirectly (the step witness counts substeps plus two and the lines share a hash); `tape_until` and `tape_count_until` in `tests/cockpit.rs` poll the wall clock up to about 30 s for a 16 KiB chunk to close. (10) Pre-existing, surfaced by Y.1: a seated body moved out of its pod by `mush_sim::unplace` plus `move_to` keeps its focus and passes `authorise_seat`, so it keeps helm, fire and now `ORDER` authority until `EXIT` (a 16c row is owed: release focus or check placement when a seated body leaves its pod by any route but `EXIT`). (11) Cosmetic: `docs/help/cockpit.md` wraps one `ORDER ALL` code span across two lines (passes `help_commands` only because its per-line tokenizer reads the tail as prose); the twelve neighbouring `legacy-handlers.toml` stanzas and `crates/mush-gateway/tests/arena_story_split.rs:124` still say twelve selectors and fourteen verbs beside the new stanza's thirteen and fifteen. *The reclaim.* (12) The `EXIT` help string in `cockpit::register` lost one alignment space in Y.3's fix round (`EXIT` plus 15 where every other verb pads to column 20; nothing pins it); `docs/help/simulator.md`'s list of what the reduced row withholds lacks `flown by doctrine`, and its `after a few seconds the tank is yours again` reads as one `BOARD` then a wait where a `BOARD` inside the floor is refused with `try again in N second(s)` and must be typed again; the doc comments of `unattended_deadline` (`lobby.rs`) and `StopReason::Unattended` (`arena/state.rs`) still reason from a one-way release; `wait_arena_pilot_left` has no callers. (13) `board`'s `Running` arm refuses only a `Stopping` record while `slot_reclaimed` requires `Running`, so a record `None` or in any other non-`Running` state would be refused only after `reclaim_seat` had bound and reseated the pilot (unreachable today: `on_match_finished` writes record and centre in one action; a one-line hardening `(Running, r) if r != Some(Running)` closes it); `SeatFacts::connected` says `BOARD takes it back` in the up-to-one-pulse window while the record is `Stopping`, where `BOARD` then answers the stopping sentence. (14) The cross-tempo script's `Released` is at the `Linkdead`'s own epoch 2 (equal, still applied), a shape production never sends since every `send_control` raises the mirror; Y.4 built on it (beats at 35, 40, 55 and 60 s, until 65 s) and it may be re-pinned to 2/3/4; the stale-`Released` purge is pinned through `apply_control` directly rather than through a `step` (phase 2 would consume the queued intent in the same pulse). (15) 16c `B-81` stays open and the reclaim relies on what it describes: the cockpit billet survives a drop, and the reclaim's activation re-takes it. *The catch-up.* (16) The `MAX_CATCHUP_*` caps are copied into `render.rs` and `cockpit.ts` (12/6/6) and nothing pins them to the arena's constants: a change to one side would let the three renderers word one wire object differently while every test stays green (the fixture's capped cases use `>=` and pass at any cap at or below 12). (17) The report's `now` figures are read in phase 1 at the `Live` (which makes them one set at every cadence; `catchup.rs` says so since the closeout) while the frame that carries them shows the end of the pulse, so at a 5,000 ms pulse a rolling tank's `cell_now` can trail the same frame's `cell` by several cells; the observer test now bounds that gap, but its `contacts_new` equality with the frame's own designators still assumes no contact changed inside the reclaim pulse (a latent flake source, not seen in the gate). (18) `at_release` is keyed by unit as the brief mandates, so two crew in one tank would share one baseline (a gunner's revival `Live` would spend the driver's snapshot); unreachable with one pilot per slot. (19) The single `contacts_more` cannot say whose overflow it is when both lists are full (`N more not named`); splitting it into two wire fields is an owner call. (20) Doc 07 §1.3 step 5's heat changes and actions taken are not in the report (per the brief), and `machine.goal` rides the wire without being said. (21) The 80 by 24 TUI golden pins only the report's tail line (`  Power online.`) by the pane's whole-line tail rule, the ring order asserted without it; re-bless at 120 columns when cut AB relaxes the layout. (22) `drop_pilot` also removes an undelivered `pending_catchup` (outside the brief), a branch unreachable through the task because `PrePulse::take` applies releases before standing controls and the same pulse's `frames()` takes any report. (23) The cross-tempo run's contact lists and rounds are empty at every cadence (the fixture tanks carry no sensor and no gun), so only `away_ms`, the cells, the wound and the machine words are compared across cadences; the `sim_cockpit` block wait uses `expect("Power ")` from the session start rather than a mark after `BOARD`. (24) The mush-server `boot_parity::stop_ledger_archive_joins_the_pass_rather_than_detaching_it` timing flake seen once in Y.2's gate and green on rerun is not on the load-sensitive list. |

### Cut L — design note (2026-09-22, before implementation)

Base: `origin/main` at `145296c2`. Scope is 16c cut L, independently of AA and B.
The operator can lift a password tempban through the store, preserve the SSH host
identity during a reset, bootstrap the first staff account without hand-written
Redis data, and distinguish authentication refusal from token cool-off. Config,
operator logs, repository documentation and CI must describe the one current game.

**Owner clarification.** The owner confirmed dev2 is unused and authorized its
removal (2026-09-22). Remove the repository's bespoke dev2 deployment/config/test
lane; another testing instance uses the shared instance template. This supersedes
Q31's prerequisite for an SSH inspection. No live instance is reset or removed by
this implementation.

**Boundaries.** L.1 owns operator/authentication paths, reset/deploy scripts and
boot diagnostics. Tempban lifts are time-limited store commands consumed once by
each connection-owning guard, preserving newer bans and permanent sitebans. Staff
bootstrap uses the existing account/store authority, validates credentials, never
prints a password/hash, and refuses a nonempty account store. Transport admission
hooks become required, and token cool-off gets its own websocket close code.
First-boot pack refusal is fatal: `on_world_loaded` already returns `ModError`, so
propagate the original cause through the normal boot firewall and fail tests at
boot rather than later on a missing room. This follows L's fail-boot option.

L.2 adds omitted config keys, warns about a disabled simulator and pod-count
clamping, detects likely misspelled top-level sections, removes dev2 and retires
the deleted ruleset-selector names. The naming-only edits precede e2e wait changes.
L.3 corrects current docs without changing the combat timing or lease contract;
three-channel K6 leases still wait for a second station (Q33).
L.4 adds strict rustdoc, built-SPA release/TLS checks and env-gate inventory, and
changes simulator waits to bounded observable conditions while preserving their
assertions. The cost run remains opt-in and requires no paid provider for CI.

**Verification.** Focused auth/CLI/store, provisioning, shipped-config and
simulator tests first; then formatting, all-target Clippy, contract gates,
`cargo test --workspace --exclude mush-bot`, the separate bot suite, the story
and harness, strict workspace rustdoc, and the built-SPA release binaries.
Linux CI verifies shell/systemd-shaped fixtures. Independent review precedes
integration; refresh main and rerun affected gates after resolving conflicts.
Existing physics/delivery findings remain AA's and are not bundled into L.

**As built (2026-09-22): L.1–L.4 complete and validated; integration record
[PR #1](https://github.com/faenix7/rustmush/pull/1).** The frozen Linux run uses `5f8353b3`; `fbe91f75` adds
six test-file corrections. Final source `ffa12f6b` also incorporates AA.3's two
production files and one test file, making the validation overlay nine files.
Detailed results and retained failures are in the
[validation report](../reviews/cut-l-2026-09-22/validation.md). `@siteban/lift` publishes a
five-minute store command with a fresh
request ID and issuance cutoff. Each connection-owning guard consumes it once,
preserving newer password failures and permanent sitebans. ID creation belongs
to the store-domain constructor, outside provider implementation files.
Admission hooks are required. Websocket token cool-off upgrades and closes with
`4429`, retaining the credential for retry; invalid credentials still use
`4401`. Match boarding and `Running` transitions log at INFO, closed SSH
connections cancel their pre-auth watchdog, disabled deliberation logs at DEBUG,
and stdout uses ANSI only on a terminal.

`--bootstrap-staff <account>` on `mushd` and `mush-server` reads a bounded
password line from redirected stdin, uses normal recovery and world boot, holds
the instance lock, starts no listener, and atomically refuses an existing account
store. Neither password nor hash is printed or accepted on argv.
`reset-instance.sh --staff` uses that path and preserves `ssh_host_ed25519`.
Successful SPA builds stamp their source commit; deploy requires that exact
commit and `index.html`, including from `git archive`.

Shipped configs expose ground-cell size, ephemeris and path-search bounds, with
required-section and explicit-value gates. Disabled TacSim logs WARN; a ceiling
above the four authored pods logs its clamp once at centre readiness. Unknown
top-level sections within two edits of known names are refused; unrelated
extensions remain allowed. Selector names became `sheet_modules` and
`onboarding_config`; obsolete fixtures and fallback parsing were removed. The
owner-authorized dev2 removal affects its repository lane only. No live instance
was reset or removed; another instance uses the shared template.

T-85 takes the fail-production-boot option: refused first-boot packs and entry
anomalies propagate their cause through the boot firewall before module boot
writes are persisted. Docs now describe the current game, eight seam messages,
current feeds, deliberation and cockpit screen zero. Strict private-item rustdoc
required the associated 132 documentation/comment fixes. T-54 is comment-only:
per-channel K6 leases still wait for a second station under Q33. CI declares
strict rustdoc, built-SPA release/TLS checks and the cost-run inventory, with an
exact-filter mock-provider step; no paid LLM provider was used.

T-41 uses one deadline covering polling and nested SSH/store reads while keeping
intentional firing cadence. Provisional observer tests select a signed test
sensor/unit pack with a 15-second classification delay; combat fixtures wait for
classified identities. Grace waits observe the exact pilot slot becoming
`left`, preserving reconnect and reflex assertions; abort journals terminate
with `aborted`. Final review also corrected the solo `CONTACTS` wait to retry
commit/control transients and require `No contacts.` or the row's `(rel `
marker. Both solo tests passed after this correction.

**Validation recorded so far.** The first workspace sweep reported 5,697 passed,
31 failed and 82 ignored across 10 failing targets. Causes were three invalid
atlas heightfield fixtures, six atlas and fourteen worldgen `road` fixtures
requiring `journey`, one provider-RNG violation, six new wait predicates, and one
panic-log fixture inheriting `RUST_LOG=warn`. Those causes were corrected.
Focused reruns passed: atlas 16, worldgen 14, and mushd simulator/log 11 with one
ignored. Separate bot tests passed 262; web tests 455; Svelte reported zero errors
and warnings; stamp tests passed two; reset/bundle shell fixtures passed.
Strict rustdoc with `-D warnings --document-private-items`, recorded all-target
Clippy and TLS Clippy, and two TypeScript-export tests with no drift passed.
Workspace all-target and TLS all-target Clippy were rerun after the final test
edits and passed. Formatting/diff checks passed. The TLS-enabled web suite passed
all 52 tests, including a real HTTPS health response and certificate-path refusal.
The exact mock-cost test passed from the compiled workspace harness with four
units over 0.1 arena minutes: two decisions issued, no refusals or errors. Its
mock-provider report was exercised; no paid provider was called.
The Windows release build passed for `mushd`, `mush-server` and `mush-gateway`
with `mushd/tls,mush-gateway/tls` at `02ead118`; the production SPA was built
first and the deploy guard confirmed its matching commit stamp.

The final workspace sweep at `b2e89da6` completed with 5,728 passed, two failed
and 82 ignored across 356 targets; both combat stories passed. The two failures
were in `ipc_authoritative_action`: pre-ownership `Busy` arrived before the
tampered-offer check, or was ignored until the ownership wait timed out. The tests
now retry the byte-identical request only before `Owned`, with 25 ms backoff
inside their existing deadlines. All ownership and rejection assertions remain;
all eight IPC tests passed in the focused rerun with normal test parallelism.
The full sweep's original two failures remain recorded here; it was not a
zero-failure run. The two final test-only edits were validated by those IPC and
solo reruns rather than repeating unaffected targets.

Both release harness tests passed with 300 units, 60 pulses, seed 2350 and the
standard 24/8-cell sensor/share reaches, in report-only mode. The first arena
measurement was p50/p95/p99/max 5.934/19.289/24.665/26.871 ms: above the cut S
12 ms p99 target, and retained as such. One repeat measured
1.339/6.867/7.249/7.528 ms with sampled host CPU at 8.9–11.9 percent. All eleven
non-timing report rows were identical between runs. The recorded cut E combat
counters match: 783 fired, 710 hits, 33 sections and 11 units destroyed, 325
blocked lines, 1,006 path queries and 87,008 path cells with zero failures,
1,435,041 ledger bytes and zero lagged pulses. The static ground baseline's p99
was 17.022 ms. No timing assertion was enabled; the quieter arena measurement is
within the 12 ms target, not a claim that the first run passed it.

**Upstream integration check.** After those results, `origin/main` advanced to
`ecaed401` (AA.1, the immobile machine). It merged cleanly at `79f8ef80`, preserving
L's rustdoc repairs and the test composition's `sheet_modules` rename. At that
merge revision, the full TacSim suite passed 877 tests with zero failures and two
ignored scale tests across 25 targets. Strict workspace private-item rustdoc
passed for all 44 crates, workspace all-target Clippy passed with warnings denied,
and formatting/diff checks passed. The two ignored release harness tests then
passed in report-only mode with the same 300-unit, 60-pulse, seed-2350 knobs:
arena p50/p95/p99/max 1.246/6.563/7.124/7.314 ms, static ground p99/max
4.101/4.713 ms. All eleven non-timing arena report rows matched the earlier
release run exactly. Timing assertions remained disabled. The three Windows TLS
release binaries above were built at `02ead118`, before this engine merge.
AA.2's boarding-deadline succession change at `66fe7361` subsequently merged at
`e7761f14`; the frozen Linux source includes both upstream cuts. AA.3's terminal
watchdog change at `61597e06` then merged cleanly at `ffa12f6b`. Source review
found no integration issue, and the matching SPA build and commit-stamp check
passed. The full TacSim suite passed 884 tests with zero failures and two ignored
across 25 targets on both Windows and Linux. Final Linux Clippy, strict workspace
rustdoc, the three TLS release binaries and both report-only harness cases passed.
AA's documentation closeout at `84154d1a` merged at `0ccf2347`,
with no production or test changes beyond `ffa12f6b`.

Overlapping workspace/release attempts
were stopped during compilation under memory pressure before tests started;
the workspace completed alone at `-j 2`. Release resumed at `-j 1`, reused the
completed `mushd` binary, and finished the other binaries at `-j 2` after a
separate build's memory pressure cleared. The configured Thin LTO was unchanged.

**Linux validation.** The owner explicitly approved uploading source for isolated
validation. The first private Redis attempt lacked JSON/search modules (10 passed,
56 failed); the same binary reached 65 passed and one genuine reader failure after
the modules were loaded. `5f8353b3` fixes `JSON.MGET` nulls hiding wrong-type module
keys; all 66 Redis tests then passed. `50347e03` fixes ShellCheck's staff-argument
finding. Provisioning and real first-staff bootstrap, authoritative boot,
quarantine/generation, kill9 durability, persistence, m10c, TLS listener,
workspace/TLS all-target Clippy and strict Linux rustdoc passed.

The frozen workspace at `5f8353b3` completed with 5,747 passed, seven failed and
82 ignored across 355 Cargo targets (356 summary rows: `mush_core` has separate
ordinary and compile-fail doctest partitions). Three failures were source-census
filters matching the checkout's own `src` directory, two were pre-ownership IPC
`Busy` races, and two were socket-buffer assumptions. Five corrected targets
passed locally: census 76, load/soak four, IPC action eight and IPC teardown one;
their all-target Clippy, formatting and diff checks passed. Separately, the
split-process recovery gate reported two passed and one failed after recovery
succeeded but SSH closed before its final banner. `6463f71d` observes gateway
completion and the exact supervision-abandoned result. These six test-file
corrections through `fbe91f75` preserve production behavior and the original
security/recovery assertions. At `ffa12f6b`, all six corrected targets passed on
Linux: 89 tests across the five workspace targets plus the three-test live-Redis
process-cut matrix. The original failed sweep remains recorded as failed.

Private build artifacts drove the validation host to 100% reported disk use
(115 MiB still free at the lowest observation; no `ENOSPC` observed). The owned
runner/bot were paused; private completed test executables were reclaimed while
source, logs and live paths were retained. Limited subsequent service/journal
checks found no restart or disk-write error; they do not prove absence of every
historical effect. Three bot E2E tests timed out during the pause; the clean
separate bot rerun passed all 262 tests. The
[validation report](../reviews/cut-l-2026-09-22/validation.md)
records the incident, exact cleanup scope, commands and outcomes.

**Final validation:** the frozen Linux aggregate was 31 passed of 34 gates;
all twelve continuation gates at `ffa12f6b` passed. Final arena harness
p50/p95/p99/max was 2.070/14.854/14.998/15.167 ms, above the 12 ms p99 target;
assertions were disabled. All 105 arena and 42 static deterministic report fields
matched the frozen run and earlier Windows baselines. The independently verified
evidence and command/count index are linked from the validation report. Private
Redis shutdown and build-cache removal were verified, restoring 183.0 GiB free
(`df` displayed `184G`); no live instance was deployed, reset or removed. GitHub jobs did not
execute because of billing/spending limits; manual validation is not a green CI
run. Cut L closes five bugs and seventeen debts, plus T-54's comment only;
per-channel leases remain open. B-81 records the retained human billet's
source-reviewed `+focus` stale-context/STARTUP gap, without a runtime full-takeover
claim. B.4 remains outside L; AA.1/AA.2/AA.3 are inherited upstream changes.

## 10. Review (2026-09-16, three independent lenses on Draft 0.1)

Three reviewers read Draft 0.1 of docs 16 and 16b against the tree: an architecture and consistency
lens, a feasibility and sequencing lens, and an adversarial lens. Thirty-five findings; every one is
dispositioned below and, where adopted, folded into Draft 0.2 of both documents. Nothing was rejected
outright; three items are owner questions or deferred.

### Critical

| # | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | The in-RAM ledger with per-pulse `moved` events would reach 100+ MB in an hour and the custody-in bundle would exceed the 256 KiB record and 16 MiB bundle ceilings, failing the whole match commit (adversarial, architecture) | **Adopted.** Ledger streamed as 16 KiB chunks through the world task to `mush_core::Ledger`; `moved` replaced by `position_summary` every 5 s; `ledger.max_bytes`, `max_unflushed_chunks`, `match.max_duration_ms`; the result record holds the digest and log id; A2 restated as "memory flat with the ledger streamed". Doc 16 §3.1, §4; cut A5, T4 |
| 2 | The arena transmitting frames through `GatewaySink` cannot resolve pilots in the split server (no `SessionManager` there) and a full channel tears down the whole gateway boundary (adversarial, feasibility, architecture) | **Adopted.** Frames are a seam message: latest-wins slots drained by the world task and emitted through its normal output path as `SendTarget::Char`; the arena has no sink; T's acceptance includes the gateway twin. Doc 16 §3.1, §5; cut T4 |
| 3 | One-jump motion tests only the destination cell, so units tunnel through buildings and water at `Quiet` and `Alert` (adversarial, architecture) | **Adopted.** The pulse is a sequence of substeps bounded to one cell of the fastest unit; per-cell tests; the invariant "no unit occupies an untested cell"; the cross-tempo property test crosses an impassable band. Doc 16 §3.1, §3.4; cut A2, A4, A7 |
| 4 | The world's `+map` frame lists every unit in the arena domain by room visibility, and staff see everything (adversarial) | **Adopted.** A deployed arena's domain is excluded from `visible_rooms`, `units_for`, `+map`, `look` and unit listings for every viewer; A1 asserts the observer rule surface by surface. Doc 16 §1, §3.6; cut S3 |
| 5 | The contact ledger cannot be "reused unchanged": it is a durable action-state family the arena cannot write, its ids are owner-derived not viewer-scoped, and the arena would silently create a second identity space (architecture, adversarial) | **Adopted.** Arena contacts are an in-RAM shadow with their own id type minted from `draw(seed, contact:<viewer>:<target>)`, never crossing to the durable family; one byte-identical refusal; the reuse claim restated as vocabulary and projection rules. Doc 16 §3.6, D-16-10; cut S2 |
| 6 | Cut R's re-point list missed eight test targets and four contract tests that import the rulesets (feasibility) | **Adopted.** R8 lists them; the four contract tests are rewrites. |
| 7 | Cut R's gate step omitted the legacy-handler stanzas and pinned baseline, the restriction-lints rows and census, the module-namespace owners rows and edges, two panic-budget files and a CI line (feasibility) | **Adopted.** R9. |

|---|---|---|
| 8 | Queued intents survive a seat release because the seam has no revocation and the arena never learns the current epoch; a pilot fires from a pod they left (adversarial, architecture) | **Adopted.** `Control` message; per-`(unit, channel)` epoch table; authorisation at apply; purge on bump; K's verify lens. Doc 16 §3.1, §3.7; cut A3, T3 |
| 9 | Committing post-panic arena state through custody-out inverts the workspace's panic firewall (adversarial) | **Adopted.** A caught panic voids the match; `CustodyIn{Void}` restores check-out snapshots and commits no arena cell. Doc 16 §3.1; §0.6 |
| 10 | Stranded `deployed` markers after a crash or routine hot restart brick every unit that was in a match; no boot sweep specified (adversarial, architecture) | **Adopted.** The marker carries the check-out snapshot; boot reconciliation in the tactics recovery pass restores, clears and releases; a restart-mid-match test. Doc 16 §3.1; cut T5 |
| 11 | The `deployed` refusal was placed at `UnitAuthorityAdapter::prepare_mutation`, which does not exist on `main` (a Codex-branch name); the real admission sites let `@destroy` and `@unit/damage` through mid-match, and one vanished unit would fail the whole check-in (adversarial) | **Adopted.** Real sites named (`TacticsDestroyGate::check`, `TacticsDetachGate::check`, `unit_damage`, `unit_remove`, `@spatial` moves); check-in per-unit fallible with `custody_lost`. Doc 16 §3.1; cut T2 |
| 12 | Check-in wrote back cells but no poses, so world placements stayed at the entry cell for the whole match and after it (adversarial) | **Adopted.** `CustodyIn` carries `final_pose`, applied through `mush_sim::place` in the same action; the placement is documented as frozen while deployed. Doc 16 §3.1; cut T2 and its verify lens |
| 13 | Checking a unit out without keeping it placed drops the pilot's channel grants (`controls_are_live` requires a placed unit), and stations keyed by unit cannot live on the pod (architecture) | **Adopted.** The world placement stays, frozen; stations and grants are on the tank; `unit.status` for a deployed unit specified. Doc 16 §3.1, §3.3, §3.6 |
| 14 | The lifecycle never named the `Boarding → Running` trigger, had no boarding verb, and put check-out in two different places (architecture) | **Adopted.** `BOARD` verb; the trigger (all seated or `match.board_timeout_ms`); check-out on that transition; pre-custody aborts release slots and pods only. Doc 16 §3.7, §3.8; cut K1, K2 |
| 15 | "Wreck" had no M32 representation, and the tanks' provenance and reset were unspecified, so "a second match can start" had no mechanism (architecture) | **Adopted.** Wreck is `hull` condition `wreck` plus a declared `RevokeCapability` set; eight persistent per-pod tanks created once by the arena pack and reset by plan at check-in and `Idle`. Doc 16 §3.3, D-16-6; cut C3, K1 |

Cut H3 |

Doc 16 §3.1, §3.5, §3.6; cut A7 |

Doc 16 §2, §3.2, §6; cut N1 |
| 22 | The flight-time formula was off by 100×, and the range the model needs was never defined (the trace's `range_cells` is Chebyshev) (architecture) | **Adopted.** `× 1000`; one Euclidean-centimetre range function; the trace supplies cover and blocking only. Doc 16 §3.2, §3.5 |
| 23 | Only the hit draw had a label; facet, burst and classification draws were undefined, and a long-lived stream violates doc 09 C5 (architecture) | **Adopted.** `draw(seed, label)` with an enumerated, contract-pinned label set; `provider_rng_policy` exemption recorded. Doc 16 §3.5; cut A5, T6 |
| 24 | The candidate cull at `max_range_cells` made any signature factor above 1 unreachable (architecture) | **Adopted.** Cull at `max_range × sensors.max_signature_factor`, declared in config and asserted. Doc 16 §2 W1, §3.6 |

### Confirmed sound by the reviewers, no change

Contextual cockpit verbs on a focused station exist (`CommandReach::Operated`, `resolve_seat`,
`channel_for`) and none of the planned verbs collide with a registered global verb; the `JoinHandle`
and two-shutdown-tail pattern is accurate (`PersistHandles`); `catch_unwind` works because `panic =
"abort"` is deliberately refused by a pinned test; the deletion estimate for the two ruleset crates
(about 63k lines) checks out.

---

## 11. Risks

| Risk | Mitigation |
|---|---|
| Cut R breaks platform tests that used WoD content as a convenience | R8 re-points, never deletes, every test that proved a platform property |
| The world task's output path becomes the arena's bottleneck at 24 pilots and 500 ms | Frames are latest-wins per pilot and dropped when stale; the harness attaches 12 sessions from the first baseline and measures fan-out |
| Custody races (a pilot leaves the pod during check-out, a staff destroy mid-match) | Check-out is one authoritative action; boarding is locked from `Boarding`; the real admission gates refuse deployed units; check-in is per-unit fallible |
| Rate independence silently violated by a later cut | A7's cross-tempo property test is mandatory for every later cut |
| The ledger stream falls behind the world task | The egress ring is bounded; `Lagged` then `Void` are named outcomes; the harness asserts bytes streamed |
| The 2.56 km ceiling is hit by content before the 10 km path lands | The arena pack is authored at 2.56 km; the 10 km addition is designed in doc 16 §11 and re-run through the harness first |
| The centre's rooms in the atlas-owned pack couple the game to `mush-atlas` content ownership | Acceptable under the single-game direction; the tanks and everything mechanical stay in `crates/tacsim/content` |
